# Handoff — 2026-10-20

## Current result

Oblivion (GOG install) now reaches gameplay on the PS4 Wine port. With music
disabled in `Oblivion.ini`, the user observed about 50–60 FPS and a sustained
run without the renderer stall seen in earlier sessions. Earlier music-enabled
runs stopped presenting while the process and audio counters continued. This
makes the music/audio path a strong lead, but it does not yet prove that
`winegstreamer` is the cause.

**Music/GStreamer investigation:** The current Wine build has no GStreamer
support: its `config.log` says the target `gstreamer-1.0`, video, audio, and
tag development packages were not found, and Wine disabled `dlls/winegstreamer`.
The package therefore has neither the WineGStreamer PE DLL nor its Unix
companion. Quartz still registers wrappers for filters that instantiate Wine's
GStreamer COM classes, explaining the observed `0x80040154` activation failures.
The existing workaround `bMusicEnabled=0` proves the game music path is
associated with the problem, not that GStreamer itself causes it. In upstream
DXVK issue #1470, disabling the `winegstreamer` override silenced messages but
the Oblivion freeze continued at the same rate; that report also reproduced
without DXVK. Meanwhile the PS4 trace measured DirectSound's mixer/padding
path running at roughly 15,000 `GetCurrentPadding` calls per second, and
disabling WASAPI event-callback initialization did not reduce that rate. Keep
these as two distinct work items: restore an actual MP3/media decode path so
music can play, and correct PS4 DirectSound scheduling so enabling music does
not destabilize the game. A DLL override alone cannot provide the missing
decoder or fix the measured mixer loop. Do not claim the freeze is fixed until
music-on gameplay is tested for several minutes.

The packaged Oct 15 runtime does include Wine's `mp3dmod.dll`, whose
implementation uses Wine's bundled `libmpg123` and registers an MP3 DMO/MFT.
The missing link is Quartz's MPEG-I splitter: `dlls/quartz/parser.c` delegates
its class creation to a WineGStreamer COM class, which is unavailable in this
build. A narrower next source investigation is whether Quartz can provide the
needed MPEG audio split path and feed the existing MP3 DMO; otherwise the
Orbis build needs a supported GStreamer runtime plus an MP3 plugin. This is
more precise than trying another `winegstreamer` override.

Use the reconstructed Oct 15 runtime as the base for this audio work. The user
confirmed that it is the stable baseline for both Oblivion and Fallout: New
Vegas, and it also runs GTA IV. Keep any audio patch separate from the newer
native-WoW64 and exception-diagnostic changes. Do not replace the active
console package or shared prefix until the audio build is ready for a deliberate
music-on test.

Turning up the game's detail caused a crash. The user relaunched and confirmed
that the game works again at the previous settings. Keep the next graphics
test incremental and change one setting at a time.

### GTA IV Vulkan memory pressure

Two consecutive `gtaiv-test` launches (native WoW64, DXVK 2.6.2-prospero2)
reached a black screen and then logged three
`DxvkGraphicsPipeline: Failed to compile pipeline: VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY`
errors, followed by `Failed to create vertex input pipeline library`. The
second run reproduced this after the user removed the app-local `dcomp.dll`:
Wine loaded `C:\windows\system32\dcomp.dll` as builtin, loaded the native
`1911.dll`, and failed the same way. This makes app-local DComp an unlikely
cause of the pipeline failure.

The runtime memory snapshots narrow the next test. On both runs, flexible
memory available fell from roughly 120–220 MiB during startup to 59 MiB just
before the Vulkan failures; on the second run `dmem` was 558 MiB at the
59-MiB snapshot and later reached 619 MiB. Wine's direct-memory allocator
reported `dmem_failures=0`, while the PS4 flexible-holder report independently
showed about 59 MiB available. Treat this as system-level memory pressure
correlated with pipeline creation, not proof that the Wine dmem allocator
failed.

The game working directory is
`C:\Games\GTAIV` (`/data/prospero-win/prefixes/gtaiv-test/drive_c/Games/GTAIV`).
DXVK logged `Using 6 compiler threads` on both failures. A controlled run with
`dxvk.numCompilerThreads = 1` confirmed DXVK read the setting, but reproduced
the same three pipeline OOM errors. At the first memory sample after DXVK
startup it had 202 MiB flexible memory free and 306 MiB dmem; about 21 seconds
later it had 58 MiB free and 584 MiB dmem, followed by OOM. It then remained
alive with `frames_put=7`, `shown=0`, 55 MiB free, and 589 MiB dmem. Reducing
compiler concurrency alone therefore did not resolve the underlying pressure.

The next config keeps one compiler thread and sets
`dxvk.enableGraphicsPipelineLibrary = False`, changing only GPL behavior
relative to the failed one-thread run. It is in the game working directory and
was read back byte-for-byte. Subsequent runs confirmed the setting was active
(`Graphics pipeline libraries not supported`), but one still logged pipeline
OOMs and later runs still stopped presenting. Disabling GPL alone is not a
fix.

With the original loading screens restored, a native run again produced audio
but no visible frames. The renderer's heartbeat remained at
`frames_put=7 shown=0`; flexible memory was 60 MiB free with 588 MiB dmem and
zero direct-memory allocation failures. At 18:07:26 it logged
`Exception on CS thread!` and `DxvkCommandList: Failed to end command buffer`.
Unlike earlier runs, this run did not log `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY` before
that failure. The app continued running with audio counters advancing but no
additional frames, so this is a persistent render/command-buffer failure, not
the same explicit pipeline-allocation error in the log.

The Wine Vulkan device reports a 661 MiB device-local heap, while the GTA
profile advertised 1536 MiB through `-availablevidmem`. That mismatch is a
testable hypothesis, not yet a proven cause. A reversible 512-MiB profile was
uploaded and readback-verified; the next comparison was run in translator mode.
The game working-directory `dxvk.conf` remains at one compiler thread with GPL
disabled.

The user switched to translator mode. `gtaiv-test` runs at 18:10:41 and
18:11:05 confirmed `cpu=translator prefix_cpu=-2` and
`-availablevidmem 512`; both log streams ended during Wine startup before any
DXVK initialization appeared. The user reported the translated run crashed,
but no new klog fault could be tied to those attempts, so their failure point
remains unknown. Because the 512-MiB test changed both CPU mode and the
advertised video memory relative to the last native baseline, it was not a
clean CPU A/B. The profile is now staged back at `-availablevidmem 1536` with
`cpu = translator`, uploaded and readback-verified (SHA-256
`38beba51c5936785ec2dc650f676eda36d6467b0faca5b117ad1b004797865d0`). This
isolates CPU mode against the 1536-MiB native baseline on the next launch. The
512-MiB translator version is saved as
`build-ps4-logs/gtaiv-test-profile-512-translator.profile`; the previous native
profile is saved as `build-ps4-logs/gtaiv-test-profile-512-native.profile`.

A later launch selected the separate `gtaiv` profile, not `gtaiv-test`. It
reported `cpu=native`, `-availablevidmem 1536`, and DLL overrides
`d3d9=n;dxgi=b` (Wine builtin DXGI). Wine then logged an unhandled read from
null at GTAIV.exe VA `0x00daf844`, before restarting the launcher. The exact
console `GTAIV.exe` was retrieved by known-path RETR and has PE image base
`0x00d90000`; the fault is RVA `0x2f844`, where the instruction is
`mov ecx,[eax]`. The surrounding code calls a COM method using IID
`7b7166ec-21c7-44ae-b21a-c9ae321ae369` (IDXGIFactory), then dereferences the
returned pointer while it is null. This points to a failed DXGI factory query
on the `gtaiv` profile, not the Vulkan memory-pressure path. Do not use that
profile for the DXVK comparison; select `gtaiv-test`, which uses native DXVK
`dxgi=n` and the test prefix. The EXE snapshot is
`build-ps4-logs/GTAIV-current.exe` (SHA-256
`409b3c973522765d5c7282555b8ea64f514528f11f695bfd0fce1d7b175c29dc`).

### DXVK command-buffer result diagnostic built

DXVK 2.6.2's `src/dxvk/dxvk_cmdlist.cpp` only checked whether
`vkEndCommandBuffer` returned success, then threw a generic message. A local
instrumentation change now includes the returned `VkResult` in that message.
The 32-bit diagnostic DLL build succeeded with two parallel jobs. Source patch:
`codex_scratch/dxvk-2.6.2-endcmd-result.patch`; output:
`codex_scratch/dxvk-diagnostic3/x32/d3d9.dll` (SHA-256
`5a254e48021652a95b0f5c3bb20992497ece575171cf4db01ab24f0ce354d077`). This is
instrumentation only; it does not fix the hang. The current console run was
still alive during deployment, so its already mapped DLL remains unchanged.
The diagnostic DLL was uploaded app-locally to
`/data/prospero-win/prefixes/gtaiv-test/drive_c/Games/GTAIV/d3d9.dll` and read
back byte-for-byte (SHA-256
`5a254e48021652a95b0f5c3bb20992497ece575171cf4db01ab24f0ce354d077`). On the
next launch it should shadow the prefix's system D3D9; verify the log loads
`C:\Games\GTAIV\d3d9.dll` before treating the instrumentation as active.
The next test retains the same native 1536-MiB profile and DXVK config; no
package rebuild is needed. The readback copy is
`build-ps4-logs/GTAIV-d3d9-command-result-readback.dll`.

The diagnostic DLL was loaded by the next native run from
`C:\\Games\\GTAIV\\d3d9.dll`. It logged `DxvkCommandList: Failed to end
command buffer: VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY` at 18:59:04, at the same time the
present count stopped advancing (`frames_put=7`, `shown=0`) while audio
continued. Wine reported about 60 MiB flexible memory free and 584 MiB dmem,
with `dmem_failures=0`. This establishes the Vulkan result, but the Wine dmem
counter does not cover every RADV host allocation and the 60 MiB value alone
does not prove global memory exhaustion.

In the PS4 RADV source, `radv_EndCommandBuffer()` can return either the result
from `radv_finalize_cmd_stream()` or the command buffer's recorded error from
`vk_command_buffer_end()`. For the AMDGPU winsys, the command-stream status can
be set to `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY` by failed `realloc()` calls for either
the command stream's BO handle list or its IB list. Both lists allocate through
this PRX's `--wrap=realloc` into Wine's shared heap. Logs were added at those
two sites, reporting which list failed, queue IP, current count/capacity, and
requested bytes; the patch is
`patches/0002-radv-command-list-allocation-diagnostics.patch`.

### Follow-up run: descriptor pool failure happens first

logged `Exception on CS thread!` followed by
`DxvkDescriptorPool: Failed to create descriptor pool`; the command-stream BO
and IB list diagnostics did not fire. `frames_put=7`, `shown=0` remained stuck
while audio advanced. The nearest memory sample was 62 MiB flexible free,
595 MiB dmem, and `dmem_failures=0`. This run reaches an earlier Vulkan failure
than the prior `vkEndCommandBuffer` OOM; the exact `vkCreateDescriptorPool`
result is still unknown. DXVK 2.6.2 uses `maxSets=24576` for its 32-bit host
pool, but that size is only a lead, not a proven cause.

The app-local 32-bit DXVK `d3d9.dll` now includes the Vulkan result and
`maxSets` in the descriptor-pool failure. It was uploaded to the same GTA IV
prefix path and read back byte-for-byte (SHA-256
`3f5afbfeac06f31fccf32793241cbe867f80b9427c8f49ce617d083db3b89e7d`). Source

coredump watcher and all three log listeners are armed for it.

### 0007 run: stalled before the descriptor-pool failure

The user started the 0007 diagnostic package at 19:30. Through 19:34:43,
there are no `RADV_DESCRIPTOR_POOL` records and no new DXVK OOM. The runtime
heartbeat is alive, but video stopped at `frames_put=7`, `shown=0`; audio
continues advancing. Memory is stable at 136 MiB flexible free and 479 MiB
dmem, with zero dmem allocation failures. CPU time, major faults, and block
I/O counters are also flat; SceShellCore's nearest VM sample reports no swap
out. This run has not reproduced the earlier
`vkCreateDescriptorPool(maxSets=24576)` failure, so it does not yet tell us
which allocation stage failed in the prior runs. The console appears stalled
before further frame submission; preserve this distinction when interpreting
the next dump or launch.

The user suspected that 0007's instrumentation caused the stall. That is
plausible as an explanation for the changed timing, but it is not the root
failure: the no-0007 A/B run reached the same seven-frame stall and then logged
`DxvkCommandList: Failed to end command buffer: VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY`
at 20:31:58, about 40 seconds after launch. At 20:32:07 it had 61 MiB
flexible free and 584 MiB dmem, with no dmem allocation failures. The app
continued running without new frames until the console was shut down from the
Power Menu at 20:32:52; no new core dump was captured. This points to 0007
changing the timing/shape of the failure, while not fixing the underlying
OOM.

For that A/B, I reversed only patch 0007 in the ignored Mesa worktree; patches
0002–0006 remain. Mesa, `libvulkan.prx`, and the app package were rebuilt. The
package was verified to contain the rebuilt PRX in both runtime locations,

`pkgout/gtaiv-radv-no-pooldiag-ab-10078/IV0000-GLDS10078_00-GLDS100780000000.pkg`

uploaded to `/data/pkg/wine-app.pkg`; GoldHEN confirmed the full transfer.

The `RADV_CMD_OOM`, `RADV_CS_FINALIZE`, and allocation diagnostics from
patches 0002–0006 did not appear in the runtime/klog captures. Inspection of

explicit settings already in that file still take precedence. See the package
details below.

## Fallout: New Vegas comparison

Fallout was selected from the launcher using the existing console profile
`fallout-new-vegas` and prefix `/data/prospero-win/prefixes/fallout-new-vegas`.
The runtime reported `cpu=native prefix_cpu=1`. It loaded `FalloutNV.exe` and
the game's native `d3d9.dll`, but produced no frames. About 21 seconds after
startup, the console recorded a `SIGSEGV` (signal 11), a user read from the
unmapped address `0x0000000fffffff40`, and Wine's `ntdll.prx` signal path. The
runtime also logged `wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create internal
thread context` before the fault. Flexible memory was still about 260 MiB and
`dmem_failures=0`, so this was not an observed memory-allocation failure.

The crash reporter completed a core dump, then deleted it during its cleanup;
the klog remains in the local ignored `build-ps4-logs/` directory. The exact
faulting game/runtime call still needs a core or better signal-context trace.
The console profile differs from the older checked-in
`src/profiles/falloutnv.profile` (`fallout-new-vegas` versus `falloutnv`), and
the console profile had no `[runtime] cpu` key. The runtime defaults PE32 DXVK
titles to native WoW64, which matches the observed `cpu=native`. A controlled
translator profile is now ready at
`/data/prospero-win/profiles/fallout-new-vegas-translator.profile`. It keeps the
same executable, existing `fallout-new-vegas` prefix, DXVK overrides, display,
and input; only the profile ID/name and explicit `[runtime] cpu = translator`
differ. It was uploaded with the minimal GoldHEN FTP client and read back
byte-for-byte. The existing package already supports this setting, so no
package rebuild was needed.

## Important runtime caveat

The translator comparison was then run. Runtime logs confirmed
`profile=fallout-new-vegas-translator` and `cpu=translator prefix_cpu=-2`.
Fallout still produced no frames and crashed with SIGSEGV. Just before the
fault, `FalloutNV.exe` attempted to create `FalloutNVLauncher.exe`; this runtime
returns `STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED` for `NtCreateUserProcess`. The following
`NtQueryInformationProcess` path faulted in syscall `0x2c` at guest address
`0x8033729a0`. DXVK initialized, memory logs showed about 246 MiB flexible free
and zero dmem allocation failures. The dump
`/user/data/sce_coredumps/GLDS10078_1792539031/GLDS10078_1792539031.sorbisdmp`
was created, then deleted by the console crash reporter before it could be
retrieved. This confirms the failure also occurs with translator selected, but
the exact underlying fault still needs a preserved dump or deeper syscall
trace. The attempted child launch is a concrete lead: determine why this
installation's `FalloutNV.exe` starts the launcher and whether the game can be
started without child-process creation before changing the runtime's general
process-creation policy.

The local `FalloutNV.exe` contains an embedded error directing users to the
launcher when the selected resolution is unsupported. The console prefix had
`Fallout.ini` but no `FalloutPrefs.ini`. The user's Windows `FalloutPrefs.ini`
is set to 1920x1080. A copy was uploaded to the PS4 prefix at
`drive_c/users/prospero/Documents/My Games/FalloutNV/FalloutPrefs.ini`; its
SHA-256 matched the source and a RETR readback. the external game drive was not modified.
The next translator run still attempted to spawn `FalloutNVLauncher.exe`,
presented zero frames, and faulted at the same address/syscall. Therefore this
preferences copy did not resolve startup. A watcher was armed for the next
`GLDS10078` coredump; the native retest below was captured before the console
crash reporter removed its source copy.

### Native retest after copying the game files

The user relaunched the same Fallout profile in native mode after copying the
game files into the install directory. Runtime logs explicitly confirmed
`profile=fallout-new-vegas`, `cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`, and emitted
`PW_NATIVE_PROFILE` records. The console copy of `FalloutNV.exe` still matches
the local source byte-for-byte (SHA-256
`a50c0e91b5d729ef10a921c32aa0cc20aac5366630056d1d60c669917a936fbb`).

The run again produced no frames. `FalloutNV.exe` attempted to create
`FalloutNVLauncher.exe`; the PS4 runtime logged that process creation is not
supported. Immediately afterward Wine repeatedly reported syscall faults in
`0x2c` and passed native `SIGSYS` through. At 16:41:40 the `orbis` thread
terminated with `SIGSEGV`: a write to `0x20` (page not present), after two
signal-fault records. The klog also reports `FAULT on a thread without Wine
data`. Flexible memory was still about 256 MiB with zero dmem failures, so this
does not look like memory exhaustion. The coredump watcher preserved
`GLDS10078_1792539653.sorbisdmp` locally under ignored `build-ps4-logs/`
(241,853 bytes; SHA-256
`5bbfb76b4e33bfe5af1611530d5757136d172484c956a7fd0c28f224e1cae050`). It has
the opaque `FACECS` header and no usable local decoder, so the dump is saved but
not yet symbolically analyzed. The live klog and ps5log contain the actionable
trace.

This confirms the native backend is active, but does not reach the game: the
launcher subprocess failure remains the earliest visible blocker, followed by
a native signal/syscall fault loop and process crash. The translator run also
failed before its first frame, so the next useful work is to trace why the game
requests the launcher and identify the `syscall 0x2c` handling path shared by
both modes. Do not treat the native crash as evidence against the native CPU
backend itself until the launcher startup path is isolated.

### Resolution-check configuration test

A read-only FTP capture of the PS4 prefix `system.reg` confirmed that the
32-bit Bethesda registration is present and points to
`C:\\Games\\FalloutNV\\`; the profile already launches
`C:\\Games\\FalloutNV\\FalloutNV.exe` directly. So the copied
install is not missing that registered path. Both PS4 `Fallout.ini` and
`FalloutPrefs.ini` had `bIgnoreResolutionCheck=0`; `FalloutPrefs.ini` already
had fullscreen 1920x1080. Changed only `bIgnoreResolutionCheck` to `1` in both
files, leaving the resolution and CPU profile unchanged. The files were read
back byte-for-byte after FTP upload. This is a targeted test for the game's
renderer-resolution guard, not a confirmed fix; the next native launch should
show whether it avoids the launcher child-process request. No package rebuild
or reinstall is needed for this profile/config-only test.

## Next useful test

### Adapter-name setting passed the earlier failure point in translator mode

The user set `sD3DDevice="PS5"` in Fallout's preferences and reported that
the next run passed the point where it had been crashing. Logs identify that
run as `profile=fallout-new-vegas-translator`, `cpu=translator prefix_cpu=-2`;
it is not evidence about native mode. No package rebuild was needed. The
checked-in `src/falloutnv-config/FalloutPrefs.ini` already contains this value,
so verify the console prefix has the same setting when comparing runs. Wine's
Vulkan trace identifies its exposed physical-device name as `PS5`, while the
separate RADV probe reports `PlayStation 4 Liverpool (RADV ORBIS) (RADV
LIVERPOOL)`. The game setting should match the adapter name Wine exposes, even
though `PS5` is not this console's hardware name.

The translator run then stopped presenting frames: it reached three frames at
320x240, then four at 1920x1080, and stayed at four. Audio-padding callbacks
continued with `playing=1` and `audible=0`. At 16:53:46 the console logged an
application crash for package `GLDS10078` (the Oblivion test package hosting
this profile). The announced dump
`GLDS10078_1792540378.sorbisdmp` had already been deleted by the crash reporter
when retrieval was attempted. This is consistent with a hang/crash, but the
audio callback counters alone do not establish that background music caused
it. The user's next launch is confirmed by logs as native:
`profile=fallout-new-vegas`, `cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`, with native stage and
profile records. Compare its frame/audio progression before trying the
Oblivion-specific music workaround. The previous launcher-child-creation fault
is no longer the immediate blocker for these runs. Do not enable general
child-process creation as a workaround; the runtime's PS4 process model remains
unimplemented. Keep the known-good Oblivion profile and music-disabled setting
intact.

### Translator level-load crash captured

The user relaunched the Fallout profile in translator mode; logs confirm
`profile=fallout-new-vegas-translator`, `cpu=translator prefix_cpu=-2`. It
reached the cutscene/level-loading transition and crashed at 17:17:45, about
4m48s after launch. GoldHEN recorded `App Crash`, reason `0xb`, for
`GLDS10078` (display name `Oblivion RX Backing Skip Quiet A-B`). The coredump
was captured before cleanup as
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10078_1792541817-171745.sorbisdmp` (914,711 bytes; SHA-256
`eb77440063214eea9d3adea1fdd325aac8c05d379cacdfe946d66add9667a51f`). The
`.sorbisdmp` has the opaque `FACECS` format; no local decoder
has been found.

Immediately before the crash, Wine repeatedly logged an illegal-instruction
fault (`0xc000001d`) at `ntdll.prx+0x9c3b9`, in syscall `0x25` (ntdll syscall
37), with return IP `6ffffff3f0f4`. This repeats the fault signature seen in
the prior translator failure. The runtime's last memory snapshot showed
`flexible_holders available=0K`, about 544 MiB mapped in the flexible pool,
`flexible_free=0K`, and `dmem` around 633 MiB; `dmem_failures` remained zero.
The process stayed alive for more than a minute after flexible memory reached
zero, so exhaustion is confirmed as pressure but not yet established as the
crash cause. A profiler sample repeatedly placed the Fallout main thread at
FalloutNV.exe VA `0x00ec6dcf`, in a bytewise string-compare loop; treat this as a
CPU hot spot to investigate, not a root cause. The saved dump and live logs
should be correlated before making another memory or syscall change.

The coredump watcher initially missed the prior run because it retried only
for 4.5 seconds, shorter than this console's roughly 6–7-second dump write.
The tracked `scripts/watch-coredump.py` now retries the exact announced path
for up to 20 seconds. This run confirms the longer window captured the dump.
Start it with `HOST PORT KLOG LOCAL_DIR TITLE_ID` before the next launch.

### Fallout translator crash trace symbolicated

The `.sorbisdmp` container itself remains undecoded, but the klog includes the
fault registers and backtrace. The first translator-side failure was
`wowprospero fault[2]`: guest `pc=0`, an attempted 4-byte write to
`0x060b1674`, `status=-8`, `insns=1`, `retired=0`; the bytes at guest PC 0 were
all zero. The translated block began `32 8b 47 34 8b 8f 50 19 00`. This is the
earliest concrete fault in the run; it is not yet explained by memory pressure.

Wine's ensuing SIGSEGV handling then crashed on the host side. The fatal
`orbis` thread had `RIP=0x800b19be3`, `R13=0`, and a write fault at `0x20`.
The module map places `ntdll.prx` at text base `0x800aa0000`, so the RIP is
`ntdll+0x79be3`. Symbolizing against the matching 10078 `ntdll.oelf` resolves
this to `server_call_unlocked`, `dlls/ntdll/unix/server.c:757`:
`data->pw_server_seq++`. `get_thread_data()` returned null on this `orbis`
thread, so exception teardown dereferenced the missing Wine thread data. The
backtrace continues through `wine_server_call` (server.c:811),
`NtTerminateProcess` (process.c:988), `send_debug_event` (thread.c:1792),
`setup_raise_exception` (signal_x86_64.c:1888), and `segv_handler`
(signal_x86_64.c:3187). Thus the Wine thread-data null dereference is the
secondary failure while handling the initial DBT fault, not the initiating
game fault.

The repeated `0xc000001d` at `ntdll+0x9c3b9` resolves to `abort_process`
(`thread.c:1645`); it is in the later process-abort path and should not be
mistaken for the first fault. The next investigation should explain why the
translator faulted at guest PC 0, then determine why the SIGSEGV handler ran
without Wine thread data. Do not change flexible-memory sizing based on this
trace alone.

### Heap allocator diagnostics and relocation question (2026-10-21)

The user asked whether GTA IV's current early hang is caused by the relocation
work, since GTA previously got farther. The current evidence points directly
to a RADV graphics-pipeline allocation failure, not a PE image relocation fault:
the failing call is the buffer-blit graphics pipeline (`src=2`, format 95,
`VK_FORMAT_R16G16B16A16_UINT`), and its 95,760-byte shader-stage-array
allocation fails while Wine reports roughly 60 MiB flexible memory free.
Relocation changes could still affect address-space layout or contribute to
aggregate memory pressure indirectly, but the observed failure is in Vulkan
pipeline construction and there is no relocation fault in this trace. The
older GTA runs also stopped presenting with audio continuing, so “GTA got
farther” describes a real behavior change, not proof that relocation caused it.

To distinguish the Wine heap's large-allocation cap from an `mmap` failure,
patch `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0071-wine64-report-heap-allocation-failure.patch`
adds the last failed request size, attempted mapping size, `errno`, and a
failure-reason code to the existing periodic memory log. Host allocator and
dmem-stat tests passed. The first Wine build attempt hit duplicate PS4 shim
symbols; rerunning with `PW_PRX_LINK=1` built the updated modules cleanly.
The PS4 module chain was rebuilt, and both staged `ntdll.prx` and
`libvulkan.prx` were verified byte-identical to their build outputs. The title
ELF contains the new `heap_failures`, `heap_last_failed`, `heap_last_map`,
`heap_last_map_errno`, and `heap_last_failure_reason` log fields.

### Wine heap direct-memory test package (2026-10-21)

The user reported that this run had failed. The logs show the process was still
alive but the renderer had stopped after seven submitted frames: RADV's
95,760-byte graphics-pipeline stage-array allocation failed with
`VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY`, and the buffer-blit pipeline then failed for
`VK_FORMAT_R16G16B16A16_UINT`. The latest runtime snapshot had 61,440 KiB of
flexible memory free, 584,368 KiB of direct memory, and 36,512 KiB mapped by
Wine's Unix heap. This was a render OOM, not a fresh application crash; there
was no new core dump.

The next package tests the relevant upstream approach from prospero-win PR
#470: Wine's Unix heap can use a platform backing for new spans and large
blocks, with anonymous `mmap` retained as fallback. The PS4 backing allocates
and maps direct memory, tracks each mapping for release, and refuses new
direct mappings when the largest available direct-memory block would fall
below a 64 MiB guard. If the availability query or allocation fails, the heap
falls back to flexible memory. `WINE_PS5_HEAP_FLEXIBLE` disables the direct
backing for an A/B control. Existing heap spans are not migrated.

The generic heap change is preserved as
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0072-wine-heap-pluggable-backing.patch`; the PS4
direct-memory implementation is in `src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c`. The
failed heap-counter diagnostic patch 0071 was reverse-applied before building,
so this package retains the existing eight-counter memory-stats ABI and does
not contain that startup regression. The heap unit test now exercises both
backing-owned unmap and fallback mmap; it passed, as did strict PS4 compilation
of the platform glue and `scripts/prx-ps4.sh`.

Package `pkgout/gtaiv-direct-heap-10078/IV0000-GLDS10078_00-GLDS100780000000.pkg`
is 444,137,472 bytes (SHA-256
`3a37c33f6cca38e67cc1ce91c308c0bd892fb3e20b24087f0e01d69f04e1e678`). Both
staged `ntdll.prx` copies match the rebuilt PRX byte-for-byte. It was uploaded
to `/data/pkg/wine-app.pkg`; GoldHEN confirmed the full transfer with FTP
`226`. Display name: **GTA IV Direct Heap Test**. Install this package for the

and `shown=0`; close that run from the PS4 menu before installing the new
package. The listeners are still running.

**Caller-backed RX retry package (2026-10-21):** Because the SIGILL run ended
before graphics initialization, the direct-heap A/B does not test the later
DXVK OOM. The klog's last actionable protection record immediately before the
fault was a caller-backed page (`0x7ffe0000`) where `sceKernelMprotect` returned
success but `sceKernelVirtualQuery` still reported read-only (`effective=0x1`).
Added a narrowly scoped fallback in `protect_exec_page()`: when the page is not
direct-memory-backed and the successful kernel request still lacks execute,
retry once through libc `mprotect`, then re-query the effective flags. The
fallback is logged as `host-retry` in build marker 0975. This preserves the
existing direct-memory migration path and does not run on pages already
reported executable.

the loaded `win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/ntdll.prx` and the
`heap_direct=0` control marker in `eboot.bin`. **GTA IV Flexible Heap RX
Retry** is at

runtime, UDP, and klog listeners are still running, and the exact-path
coredump watcher is armed for GLDS10078.

**Caller-backed RX retry result (2026-10-21, 08:43):** The user installed and
launched `GTA IV Flexible Heap RX Retry`. Runtime confirms `cpu=native`,
`profile=gtaiv-test`, and `heap_direct=0 (anonymous mmap control)`. It again
crashed before `GTAIV.exe` or DXVK started. The klog repeats the exact startup
fault seen in the earlier direct-heap run: `SIGILL`, user-read page fault at
`0x100010b5e8`, RIP `0x80000009fe` in `libkernel.sprx`, `RSP=0x100010b5e8`,
`RDI=0xb`, `RSI=0x100000ffb0`, and `R12/R13=0x100000000`. The process had
reached `server_init_process_done begin` 747 ms earlier. The run's dump was
captured as `build-ps4-logs/GLDS10078_1792597409.sorbisdmp` (177,596 bytes;
SHA-256 `5ee74af0bab26f1d5ac92b0818269117e1be369e4c7d673c789f8605648db980`);
it has the same opaque `FACECS` container and no local decoder.

The RX fallback did not change the outcome: the final checks for pages
`0x220000` and `0x7ffe0000` still report `protect=0`, `query=0`,
`effective=0x1`, and `host-retry=0` (libc `mprotect` also returned success
without changing the queried permissions). This is not evidence that those
pages caused the stack fault. Do not try more permission-flag variants.

**PR #470 comparison:** [PR #470](https://github.com/mpereiraesaa/prospero-win/pull/470)
keeps Wine's Unix-side heap in direct memory to conserve flexible memory for
the title and graphics stack. The direct-memory path was active in the earlier
08:11 run (`Unix heap prefers direct memory`); it produced the identical
`0x100010b5e8` / `libkernel+0x9fe` crash. The 08:24 heap-control run and this
08:43 run explicitly used anonymous-mmap backing and produced the same fault.
So both heap modes are now tested against this **pre-game startup crash**;
heap backing is not its cause. PR #470 remains relevant to the separate,
later DXVK/RADV flexible-memory pressure and OOM issue, which these early
crashes never reach.

The next useful build should add no-behavior-change progress markers inside
`server_init_process_done()` around `signal_init_process()`, `init_teb_data()`,
the wineserver `init_process_done` request, and `signal_start_thread()`. The
existing Wine marker only says that this function began; the crash happens
before any GTA/WoW64 guest execution and leaves several startup steps
unlocalized. Keep the existing native profile; heap mode can stay on the
anonymous control, and avoid another RX or heap A/B. The UDP/runtime/klog
listeners remain active; the exact-path GLDS10078 coredump watcher was
rearmed at `build-ps4-logs/coredump-watch-20261021-0855.log`.

### Native startup boundary trace package built and uploaded

To isolate the repeat fault without changing startup behavior, added opt-in
`WINE_PS5_TRACE_STARTUP` markers around `server_init_process_done()` and
`signal_init_process()`. The trace records entry/exit for device-info and cwd
handling, signal initialization, TEB setup, the wineserver `init_process_done`
request, and guest-thread handoff. It also records entry and return for each
native signal registration (including the signal number and result). The
source patch is
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0074-ntdll-trace-ps4-startup-handoff.patch`;
reverse application against the patched Wine source was verified. Existing
profile startup tracing already sets `WINE_PS5_TRACE_STARTUP=1`.

The rebuilt ntdll PRX contains the new marker strings. Full PRX rebuild and
the relocatable package build both succeeded. The package retains the native
`gtaiv-test` profile, the anonymous-mmap heap control, and the relocation fix;
it contains `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx`. **GTA IV Startup Trace** is
`pkgout/gtaiv-startup-trace-10078/IV0000-GLDS10078_00-GLDS100780000000.pkg`
(445,513,728 bytes; SHA-256
`d814f410dd7c4ceda6b672c25a4ab3436e54cae4095fce2ec0d072ef85079d62`). It was
uploaded to `/data/pkg/wine-app.pkg`; GoldHEN FTP confirmed the full byte count
and returned `226`. The user should update the existing `GLDS10078` install,
then launch `gtaiv-test`. Runtime, UDP, and klog listeners remain running, as
does the exact-path crash-dump watcher. Read the last `wine startup:` marker
and the last `native_signal sigaction begin`/`done` pair to locate the stage
where execution stops; do not infer a faulting syscall from the earlier RX
records alone.

### Initial guest-thread stack fault and follow-up package (2026-10-21)

The 08:57 launch reached every `signal_init_process()` checkpoint, completed
`init_teb_data()` and the wineserver `init_process_done` request, then stopped
at `server_init_process_done signal_start_thread begin`. Klog reported a fatal
SIGILL at `libkernel.sprx+0x9fe`, with a user read fault at `0x100010b5e8`;
RSP was the same address and the page was not present. The dump was captured
as `build-ps4-logs/GLDS10078_1792598234-085715.sorbisdmp` (179,846 bytes,
SHA-256 `b5694b7296862f945dc636333bdf3a54e44389cadbcd03ad893b651eb5ce6790`).
The `.sorbisdmp` container is still opaque, but the klog contains the complete
fault registers.

To identify the exact instruction, the matching console file
`/system/common/lib/libkernel.sprx` was retrieved read-only to
`codex_scratch/libkernel-ps4-current.sprx` (SHA-256
`8b8c1c2f7a9eb84ae583944bf3a389bb4a64ded0058ad745c238477e766ab56a`).
Disassembly at offset `0x9f0` shows a libkernel syscall stub (`mov rax,0x49`,
`syscall`, then `ret`); the faulting RIP is that `ret`, trying to read its
return address from the unmapped address currently in RSP. This identifies a
bad/missing thread-stack return word at the first-thread handoff. It does not
yet identify which setup step produced the stack address, so do not change heap
backing, RX protection, or PR #470 for this fault.

**Active-stack mprotect trace result (2026-10-21, 10:55):** The range trace
confirmed the faulting operation. Wine requested `prot=0x3` for view 7, and
`mprotect_exec()` added execute, so the PS4 glue entered
`pw_wine_dmem_protect()` for the whole direct-memory range with `prot=0x7`.
The thread's faulting `RSP` (`0x100010b5d8`) lies inside that exact view
(`0x1000000000` through `0x1000110000`). The `sceKernelMprotect` call did not
return; klog again shows `SIGILL` at `libkernel+0x9fe` and a missing stack
page. This is direct evidence that the force-exec walk is changing protection
on the stack it is actively using, rather than a generic WoW64 syscall issue.
Dump: `build-ps4-logs/GLDS10078_1792605305.sorbisdmp` (192,189 bytes; SHA-256
`d738fee6b9c311cb44c4a00c24a70b1b89451052d2cab855b3672e8ed942d3eb`).

**2026-10-21 result — active-stack skip passed; later native syscall crash:**
The 10078 launch logged `skip active stack view[7]` for the view containing
the current stack, then completed views 8–11 and returned from
`ProcessExecuteFlags` at 11:01:30. The user confirms it advanced through the
cutscene load to the GTA menu, then crashed while loading the cutscene. This
is a real advance past the previous force-exec SIGILL.

GoldHEN reported `App Crash`, reason `0x4`, at 11:04:07. The watcher captured
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10078_1792605846.sorbisdmp` (212,174 bytes; SHA-256
`d906bd491b06d85522de04878dc5a3c5331b978950b1e70dce0d32dd8faa1b8c`). The
runtime's last fault sequence is a repeated privileged-instruction exception
at `ntdll.prx+0x9dc39` with bytes `0f 0b` (`UD2`), while in ntdll syscall
`0x25` / `NtQueryInformationThread`; its SIGSYS has `RAX=0xffffffff8b16aeb1`
and is passed through, then Wine repeatedly enters the same exception path
until `virtual_setup_exception` reports stack overflow and a nested exception
on the signal stack. This sequence is secondary crash reporting; the first
fault identified below is the initiating failure for this GTA run.

Do not confuse this with the older GTA failures that were resolved before the
relocation work. The October 10–15 handoff history records two distinct
milestones: the DirectComposition/DXGI composition-swapchain fixes (patches
0879 and 0860) got the 1911 startup path working, and the established GTA
profile on base Liverpool (no `--neo`) passed the intro cutscene. The October
14 status also records the split runtime loading the established GTA profile
into the game. Neo's separate GPU hang at that cutscene remained unresolved.
The original `gtaiv.profile` selected `cpu=translator`; this test explicitly
logged `profile=gtaiv-test, cpu=native`. So the current failure is a regression
on the newly enabled native WoW64 route, after those earlier GTA startup and
base-GPU milestones. Preserve the known-good base/translator combination as
the control, and debug this run at the native syscall/exception boundary
rather than repeating the old Mesa/Neo experiment. The existing signal
diagnostics include 0937's native SIGSYS pass-through and 0945's
privileged-opcode classification.

**Correction after resolving the first fault (2026-10-21):** The GTA first
fault is an unhandled read at `0x7AFE167D`, reading address `0x24`. The loaded
module map identifies the address as app-local DXVK 2.6.2 `d3d9.dll` RVA
`0x4167D`. Disassembly shows `D3D9CommonTexture` calling
`D3D9DeviceEx::LookupFormat`, which dereferences the device's adapter pointer;
the read at `0x24` means that pointer is null. The later `winedbg` process
creation failure (`NtCreateUserProcess` unsupported) and repeated
`NtQueryInformationThread` SIGSYS/UD2 sequence are secondary failure reporting,
not the first fault. The Boost Mode A/B run produced the same crash, ruling
Boost Mode out.

one-time D3D9 device/adapter pointer log and a guarded null-adapter diagnostic
at format lookup. The guard throws a normal DXVK error instead of faulting at
`0x24`, so the next log should identify the device address and requested
format. Its 32-bit DLL is
`codex_scratch/dxvk-diagnostic4-d3d9-null-adapter.dll` (SHA-256
`2f70d2f3fca0dd2d5248d5e45dbcad78ecea5b7434de2c40a5c227d3cbce7f93`), uploaded

back with an identical hash. No package reinstall is required. The
GLDS10078-specific dump watcher and focused DXVK fault log watch are running;
the runtime, UDP, and klog receivers remain up.

**Diagnostic7 uploaded (2026-10-21, 12:56):** The 2.6.2 fault at RVA
`0x4249D` is in the constructor's `m_image->getMemoryInfo().size` path. The
machine code loads `DxvkImage::m_storage` and then reads its memory-info size;
the null `+0x24` read is consistent with missing backing storage. Diagnostic7
adds a `hasStorage()` check immediately before that call. If storage is absent,
it logs the texture/image/device addresses, D3D and Vulkan formats, pool,
usage, dimensions, layer/mip counts, and backbuffer status, then throws a
DXVK error instead of crashing. This is a targeted diagnostic, not yet a fix
for why storage would be missing.

It replaced only the app-local D3D9 DLL at

The guest PCs now map to concrete Wine stubs. `7bf2d2b0` is the return site in
32-bit `ntdll!NtWaitForSingleObject`; `7937d7de` and nearby samples are in
`mmdevapi!client_GetCurrentPadding`; these may describe audio/wait activity,

that syscall a stub (`win32u.spec`, `user32.spec`). One thread accumulated over
21 million syscall transitions in the run. This is a strong lead, not yet
proof that the stub causes the stall: the sampler records the stub's return
address, not its caller. Patch `0085-wow64native-sample-syscall-callers.patch`
now captures the guest stack return address alongside each sampled syscall
PC, so the next run can identify which module is repeatedly calling it. The
current run was still alive at the last log check; close it before installing
or running another package.

**NEO failure localized; Liverpool exception-context package uploaded (2026-10-21, 13:35–13:42):** The user ran the existing NEO `gtaiv-test` package. It reached the GTA IV loading image, then Wine logged an unhandled 32-bit read at address `0x10`, instruction address `0x00D9EB84` (`GTAIV.exe+0xEB84`). Disassembly shows `mov ecx, DWORD PTR [edi+0x10]`; the effective address means `EDI` was zero, so this is a null-object dereference in the game. Wine could not launch `winedbg` (`NtCreateUserProcess process creation is not supported on PS5`), and the app returned to its launcher while the TV could still show the last frame. Memory peaked at 916,608 KiB dmem, flexible memory fell to 50,176 KiB, but `dmem_failures=0`; memory pressure is present, not yet shown to cause the null pointer. The relevant source log is `build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261021-130250.log`; klog is `build-ps4-logs/ps4-klog-20261021-130250-watch.log`.

The next test is deliberately **base Liverpool, not NEO**. Patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0962-kernelbase-log-x86-exception-context.patch` adds a bounded `PW_EXCEPTION32` register and stack log at Wine's unhandled-exception path, where winedbg cannot start on this console. Package: `pkgout/gtaiv-wow64-exception-context-10078-base/IV0000-GLDS10078_00-GLDS100780000000.pkg`; SHA-256 `706ca2ba1a98e6c933d5aebfcebf4532b9194371c779e8baad9764cf5bcd270f`. Added `dcomp=n` to `src/profiles/gtaiv-test.profile`, uploaded `/data/prospero-win/profiles/gtaiv-test.profile`, and verified it by direct RETR readback. The next launch logged the new override, but failed before DComp loaded: Wine reported `Library dcomp.dll (which is needed by C:\Games\GTAIV\1911.dll) not found`, followed by an unhandled read at address `0x0`; `1911.dll` was present, but its DComp dependency was not. The existing `dcomp.dll.bak` was then retrieved from the GTA IV root (61,454-byte i386 PE, SHA-256 `18bc9b24b6dde6a6fd52e3691311f229c2049a41d6768c064c9f6ebcbf01927a`, identical to this build's Wine host DComp), and renamed remotely to `dcomp.dll` with FTP RNFR/RNTO success. Next launch should confirm `C:\Games\GTAIV\dcomp.dll` loads as native and allow the original 1911 startup path to continue. The existing `dxgi=n` setting remains unchanged for this test; historical findings note 1911 once failed on Wine's private DXGI interface with native DXGI, so change DXGI separately if the next failure points there.

Keep the app-local file in place, but let Wine resolve DComp as builtin on the next test. This should distinguish the original `1911.dll` startup behavior from the broken override configuration.

**Fallout NV crash capture and Oblivion relocation correction (2026-10-21, 15:05–15:12):** Fallout New Vegas loaded into gameplay on profile `fallout-new-vegas`, with runtime `cpu=native prefix_cpu=1`, then crashed at 15:05:41. The exact-path coredump watcher captured `build-ps4-logs/GLDS10078_1792620342.sorbisdmp` (320,570 bytes; SHA-256 `48e78f6772e075246aba9565368d4dffdbe59e3303b824e02a3051da8d751897`). Flexible memory was at 0 KiB immediately before the fault, but dmem was 120,288 KiB and `dmem_failures=0`; this is memory pressure, not evidence of an allocation failure. The first logged error was `map_file_into_view unaligned shared mapping 24b00000-24c01000 not supported`, followed by `virtual_map_section mapping 24b00000 101000 910000 failed` and `Mapping non-persisted file failed: 87`. Wine then faulted on a null read at `ucrtbase.dll` VA `0x7B9A68CE` (loaded base `0x7B990000`, RVA `0x168CE`). Disassembly of the available i386 UCRTBase at that RVA is a vectorized copy (`movdqu` from `[esi]`); the first-fault guest registers were not printed, so a null source is likely but not yet confirmed. The subsequent `winevulkan.so+0x3a9a0` syscall fault, SIGSYS loop, and signal-stack overflow are secondary exception-handler failures. The FACECS dump remains opaque; retain it for any decoder work.

The user then launched Oblivion on the just-tested package. Runtime selected `cpu=native prefix_cpu=1` and initialized `wow64native.dll` successfully, but Wine exited before the game window with `failed to create main module for C:\\Games\\OblivionGOG\\Oblivion.exe, status c0000018` (`STATUS_CONFLICTING_ADDRESSES`). Inspection confirmed the package stage used for this test did **not** contain `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx`. This is the known fixed-base collision for Oblivion's non-relocatable PE32 executable; it confirms the package lacked the required relocation handoff, rather than disproving the native CPU path. `scripts/build-app-ps4.sh` explicitly documents that this ordinary low-address eboot collides with Oblivion at `0x400000`; use `scripts/build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh` for Oblivion.

The corrected Liverpool relocatable package was rebuilt with the current source and pushed to `/data/pkg/wine-app.pkg`: `pkgout/orbis-win-relocatable-10078/IV0000-GLDS10078_00-GLDS100780000000.pkg` (445,513,728 bytes; SHA-256 `cbde91d390d389e91484442a978e94cc392d176810cf9834d3aa2f4fa1b87199`). Its staged `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx` is present (SHA-256 `544d875a4dd9c10fbc1d8318f1f8aa953180c8c0520f4233a94be43d59e77f94`), and `eboot.bin` is the low-address handoff stub. GoldHEN FTP confirmed all 445,513,728 bytes transferred with `226`. This package retains current ABI-v3 native WoW64 and current file-system/input port code, uses title ID GLDS10078, and is not NEO. The profile already selects native CPU. Install this package before evaluating whether Oblivion still works on the current code. The UDP, ps5logd, and klog listeners remain running; the exact-path coredump watcher has been re-armed for the next GLDS10078 crash.

**Fallout NV fault decoded; object-region diagnostic ready (2026-10-21, 15:24):** The corrected logger captured the next startup fault in `build-ps4-logs/GLDS10078_1792621459.sorbisdmp` (286,952 bytes; SHA-256 `153b115a8dd30565356f05d4f31dbc97abde71e8c501a5ebcbd4542377a61663`). This is again a startup crash, but the exception differs from the earlier read at `0x004A8A90`: it is a write fault to `0x3002CD24` at EIP `0x7BE8FC49`. Loaded `kernel32.dll` is based at `0x7BE80000`; disassembly identifies EIP as `InterlockedIncrement`, called from `FalloutNV.exe+0x65E7A4` (return address `0x00A5E7AA`). The caller reads an object pointer from its array at index `0x153` and increments the word at object+4 (`ESI=0x3002CD20`, refcount pointer `EBX/EDX=0x3002CD24`). This resembles a Gamebryo reference-counted object, but the object's allocation/protection state is not yet known. At the preceding 5-second sample, flexible memory had dropped to 32,768 KiB, dmem was 156,784 KiB (peak 179,744 KiB), and `dmem_failures=0`; low remaining flexible memory is correlated with this run but not proven to cause the write fault.

Patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0963-kernelbase-log-faulting-object-memory.patch` adds `VirtualQuery` and a bounded two-word `ReadProcessMemory` sample for the x86 `ESI` object. Both diagnostic patches apply in order to a clean Wine source, and the new i386 DLL builds with all `PW_EXCEPTION32 object` strings. A relocatable Liverpool package is ready at `pkgout/orbis-win-relocatable-objectdiag-10078/IV0000-GLDS10078_00-GLDS100780000000.pkg` (449,052,672 bytes; SHA-256 `4a81cf900ef21fc7dace889edd8fe74a3c377966a22fb0511630522a1629ca09`). After the console returned, it was uploaded to `/data/pkg/wine-app.pkg`; GoldHEN confirmed all 449,052,672 bytes with `226`. UDP, ps5logd, reconnecting klog, and the exact-path GLDS10078 dump watcher are running again. Install this package and launch Fallout NV once to capture the object's page state and first two words.

**Fallout NV object diagnostic captured; crash is a null vtable (2026-10-21, 15:37–15:40):** The run used the objectdiag Liverpool package and selected native CPU (`cpu=native prefix_cpu=0`), profile `fallout-new-vegas`; the game advanced through its cutscene toward gameplay. At 15:40:43 Wine logged an unhandled read of `0x8C` at Fallout NV EIP `0x00A59D5E`, with `EAX=0`, `EDI=0x3204CEE0`. The new `VirtualQuery` sample classified address zero as `MEM_FREE` (`state=0x10000`, `protect=PAGE_NOACCESS`); the bounded read at zero predictably failed with error 299. This is a null-vtable dereference, not evidence that the object lives in an unmapped/low flexible-memory region.

The exact executable was retrieved from the known prefix path to `build-ps4-logs/FalloutNV.exe` (16,079,360 bytes). Disassembly at `0x00A59D5E` is `mov edx,[eax+0x8c]`; immediately before it, Fallout code does `mov eax,[edi]`, so the pointer stored at `[0x3204CEE0]` was zero. The function at `0x00A59D30` iterates an object/list obtained through `[ecx+0x24]` and dispatches through each candidate's vtable. Its caller at `0x0056942D` is followed by the return address `0x00569432`. This localizes the immediate failure to a null vtable in a candidate object; why that candidate is null/selected remains unresolved. Flexible memory was still 29,696 KiB free, dmem 156,064 KiB (peak 179,600 KiB), with zero dmem failures, which again does not establish OOM as the cause.

After this first fault, Wine could not start `winedbg`; repeated native `SIGSYS` faults culminated in a Wine stack-overflow message at 15:40:44. The PS5 log is `build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261021-153138.log`. The reconnecting klog receiver was stale during this run (its file still ended at 15:32), so this crash's sorbis dump was not captured. The user then launched `oblivion-gog` on the same package and confirmed Oblivion still works. The klog receiver was refreshed; current listeners are PID 4136804 (UDP), 4136809/4177453 (klog supervisor/receiver), 4136816 (ps5logd), and 4136818 (dump watcher). A fresh filtered tail is session 11119.

**Fallout NV failure decoded further; next candidate-object probe built and pushed (2026-10-21, 15:42–15:49):** The user confirmed Oblivion still works on the objectdiag package. `FalloutNV.exe` was retrieved from the known prefix path to `build-ps4-logs/FalloutNV.exe` (16,079,360 bytes) and disassembled with `objdump`. At `FalloutNV.exe+0x659D5E`, the faulting instruction is `mov edx,[eax+0x8c]`; immediately before it, the game executes `mov eax,[edi]`. Thus the candidate at `EDI=0x3204CEE0` has a null first word/vtable, and the dereference of `0x8c` faults at `EAX=0`. The enclosing function at `0x00A59D30` walks a list via `[ecx+0x24]`, reads candidate pointers from `[esi+8]`, and dispatches through their vtables. Its caller at `0x0056942D` passes the list owner in ECX and follows with the return site `0x00569432`. We know this candidate pointer was non-null; its allocation region and remaining fields were not yet sampled. This is a corrupt or incomplete object/list candidate hypothesis, not yet an explanation for why the game produced it.

New patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0964-kernelbase-log-x86-candidate-object.patch` extends the bounded exception diagnostic to query EDI's region and read the first four DWORDs. The ordered 0962→0963→0964 patch check passed, and the i386 kernelbase DLL built with the new `PW_EXCEPTION32 candidate` strings (SHA-256 `d14d4dcd23ba9f90601dfa4cc21e0f1d7706ff33a8e601d9ae61fd6a206f225a`). It was uploaded to `/data/pkg/wine-app.pkg` and GoldHEN confirmed all bytes with `226`. It retains title ID GLDS10078, ABI-v3 native WoW64, and the relocation PRX. The user should install it and launch Fallout NV; the next exception should report `PW_EXCEPTION32 candidate` with EDI's state and contents. The listeners remain running; do not stop them unless the user says to.

The user observes that this and other package builds take a long time before frames appear, but eventually render. Treat this as a shared startup/presentation latency issue, distinct from Fallout's title-specific null-vtable fault. The Oblivion run that validated the current diagnostic package also showed a long period at four frames while input, audio, page faults, and file reads continued; later the user confirmed Oblivion works. Gather a clear first-frame/steady-state timeline on future runs, and don't attribute the shared delay to Fallout's object failure.

**Oct 15 build A/B: faster startup and reaches gameplay (2026-10-21, 15:52):** The user switched from the candidate-object diagnostic package to the reconstructed Oct 15 package, observed much faster frame loading, and confirmed that this run reached gameplay. Compare the complete package/build differences, especially always-on logging and runtime/library changes, before assigning cause. ## 2026-10-22 — Oct 15 Liverpool native-start diagnostic for Titan Quest

The Titan Quest Anniversary run selected `titan-quest-anniversary` with
`cpu=native`, but Wine logged `wow64native.dll did not start (c00000bb);
using wowprospero.dll`. Native WoW64 therefore did not start; the game ran on
the translator. The same run had `frames_put=0`, no audio, and
`err:wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create internal thread context`.
At the memory sample, flexible memory was 335,872 KiB, dmem was 133,376 KiB,
and dmem allocation failures were zero. The log disconnected without a captured
crash, so keep backend startup and the no-frame graphics failure as separate
questions.

Added patch `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0086-wow64native-report-init-rejection-reason.patch`. The native backend now reports whether the signal-capability query is missing, which required capability bits are missing, or that all required bits were accepted. It leaves the safety gate and translator fallback unchanged. The PS4 Unix companion was rebuilt against the Oct 15 Wine build; its `ntdll.prx` is byte-identical to the Oct 15 package stage. The host WoW64 PE companion was rebuilt from the matching pinned Wine source.

Built and uploaded package `pkgout/oct15-native-rejectdiag-10081/IV0000-GLDS10081_00-GLDS100810000000.pkg` to `/data/pkg/oct15-native-rejectdiag-10081.pkg`. SHA-256: `62d62ad2b89750f1b436bfeadfb0d01ccfd6dc8b4e7ec8d339ecf4d1891f0e71`; FTP confirmed all 446,693,376 bytes with `226`. It is based on `pkgout/oct15-translator-reloc/pkg-stage`, preserves the Oct 15 EBOOT and relocation PRX, and changes only the native WoW64 PE/PRX pair plus package title metadata. SFO `ATTRIBUTE=0`, so this is Liverpool/base mode, not NEO.

Install GLDS10081 and launch the existing Titan Quest Anniversary profile. It already requests native CPU; no profile change or reinstall of the game prefix is needed. Look for `PW_NATIVE_INIT rejected reason=signal_query_missing` or `PW_NATIVE_INIT rejected reason=signal_caps ... missing=...`. An accepted message means the backend initialized, but does not by itself prove the game rendered. The no-frame/D3D9 swapchain issue remains unresolved; the current profile is windowed, so DXVK PR #5726's fullscreen invalid-mode fix is not yet established as relevant. Runtime, UDP, and klog listeners remain running.

**GLDS10081 Titan Quest run and corrected native-provider package (2026-10-22):** The latest capture reports `PW_NATIVE_INIT rejected reason=signal_query_missing symbol=__wine_prospero_native_wow64_caps version=1`, then `wow64native.dll did not start (c00000bb); using wowprospero.dll`. This proves GLDS10081 did not enter native WoW64. On the observed attempts Titan Quest never submitted a frame or audio; Wine logged `err:wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create internal thread context`, then the log connection disconnected. There is no captured Wine exception or coredump, so the user's report that it crashed is consistent with the run ending but its exact termination is not established. The existing dump watcher was still filtered for GLDS10078 and would have missed a GLDS10081 dump.

Do not credit that DXVK change for the present result.

**Second GLDS10082 launch crash captured and ntdll fault symbolized (2026-10-22):**
The next launch crashed at 06:21:48 PDT. The repaired watcher retrieved the exact
GoldHEN dump before cleanup:
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10082_1792675309.sorbisdmp` (185,939 bytes; SHA-256
`4ecf35e6e22b4e3c05b0be6b247386424a13141c8f525554aa3d2378bad6ca93`). This
FACECS v2 container is still opaque to local tools. Klog and runtime logs retain
the actionable context: native WoW64 was again rejected with
`signal_query_missing`, then `wowprospero.dll` fallback ran; the game produced
zero frames and Wine reported `wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create
internal thread context`.

The fatal address was `ntdll.prx+0x77eec` (RIP `0x800b17eec`), reading `0x1c`
with `R13=0`. The exact matching Oct 15 PRX stub ELF and DWARF map this to
`server_call_unlocked()` in `dlls/ntdll/unix/server.c:686`; the instruction is
`mov 0x1c(%r13),%ebx`, reading `thread_data->tid` after
`get_thread_data()`. The saved backtrace resolves through `wine_server_call`
(server.c:722), `NtTerminateProcess` (process.c:988), `send_debug_event`
(thread.c:1750), `setup_raise_exception` (signal_x86_64.c:1846), and
`segv_handler` (signal_x86_64.c:2928). This places the recorded null dereference
in Wine's exception-reporting path on a thread without Wine thread data; it may
be secondary to an earlier fault, whose original context is not established by
the text logs. Treat the thread-data/exception path as the lead, not a DXVK
fullscreen issue or memory exhaustion. A fresh GLDS10082 watcher is already
armed for another run.

**GLDS10082 crash decoded; native capability lookup omission fixed in next package (2026-10-22):**
The captured run again rejected native WoW64 with `signal_query_missing`, so
Titan Quest ran on `wowprospero.dll`. It created no frames or audio; Wine logged
`wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create internal thread context`, then
faulted in `ntdll.prx+0x77eec` while reporting an exception. The faulting
instruction dereferenced `thread_data->tid` through a null `R13` in
`server_call_unlocked()`. This may be secondary to an earlier fault; the dump
container is saved but opaque to the available decoder. Flexible memory was
still plentiful, with no dmem allocation failures.

The missing native signal capability has a concrete build cause. The manually
built `wow64native.prx` was omitted from the normal Unix-library shim loop and
did not link `pw_wine_dl_libc.o`. Consequently its `dlsym(RTLD_DEFAULT, ... )`
resolved to the OpenOrbis libc stub, which returns NULL, even though the
packaged `ntdll.prx` exports `__wine_prospero_native_wow64_caps`. Added the
Wine dlsym-forwarding shim and ntdll PRX stubs to the `wow64native` link in
`prospero-win-orbis-upstream/tools/build_wine_orbis_prx.sh`. The rebuilt PRX
now imports `pw_wine_dl_sym` from ntdll and its `dlsym` wrapper forwards to the
shared Wine module registry; this was verified with `readelf` and disassembly.

Built separate Liverpool package GLDS10083 from the GLDS10082 stage, replacing
both copies of `wow64native.prx` only. Both staged copies match SHA-256
`383447444b3a166dab67a82324a247362b461cd73501b6546be22e7e91014ed6`.
Package:
`pkgout/oct15-native-dlsym-10083/IV0000-GLDS10083_00-GLDS100830000000.pkg`,
446,693,376 bytes, SHA-256
`caaa08feca9afae952448fced6cabda05ba0b609cf3da7bf85466245a4cc903d`.
All 1,785 staged payload files were hash-verified. Uploaded to
`/data/pkg/oct15-native-dlsym-10083.pkg`; GoldHEN returned `226` for the full
transfer. Install GLDS10083 and launch the existing Titan Quest profile. The
first check is `PW_NATIVE_PROVIDER caps=7` followed by
`PW_NATIVE_INIT accepted`; only then will the run exercise native WoW64. A new

listeners remain running.

**GLDS10083 Titan Quest crash and profile compatibility correction (2026-10-22):**
The GLDS10083 launch did initialize native WoW64:
`PW_NATIVE_INIT accepted version=1 caps=0x00000007 required=0x00000007`,
followed by `wow64native initialized: cs32=0033 ss32=003b cs64=0043 avx=1`.
`TQ.exe` loaded at `0x00400000`, but the title submitted no frames or audio
before the crash. Wine also reported

The coredump watcher successfully retrieved
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10083_1792676122.sorbisdmp` (134,350 bytes; SHA-256
`f9a47158453c6da3b059d4f786c825da5b1c6dfde8a23360d105a9f92256bab7`).
Klog records SIGSEGV in `libkernel.sprx` at offset `0xc742`, reading `0x1f88`;
the instruction is `movl (%rax), %eax`. Its caller obtained that pointer
through a helper that reads `%fs:0x10`; at the fault `%r13` held `0x1e00`,
while Wine's guest TEB was `0x7ffdd000`. This points to a broken PS4 host
thread/TLS context during native WoW64 startup, but does not yet prove which
transition corrupted it. The crash is after successful capability negotiation,
so do not revert the dlsym fix or count it as another native-init rejection.

GLDS10083 had reused the older app PRX from GLDS10082. The EBOOT hashes happen
to match, which initially obscured the mixed payload. Built GLDS10084 from
the GLDS10083 stage, replacing only `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx` with
the verified GLDS10080 copy (SHA-256
`c8f48eb0fc5d9d8bb149fb1181bdc57cb39ebbfa07d66d9cf26c89ec77334da2`). This

The crash's libkernel arguments identify signal 10 (SIGBUS) on the failing
thread. The earlier ORBW00007 probe registered SIGBUS but only delivered two
SIGTRAPs, so the signal path implicated here had not actually been measured.
Added a focused ORBW00009 probe that asynchronously sends SIGBUS to its own
thread while that thread runs in 32-bit compatibility mode, with a signal
entry that restores host FS before C and checks the saved guest FS/frame on
return. Package
`pkgout/orbis-native32-sigbus-probe/IV0000-ORBW00009_00-ORBW000090000000.pkg`
is 6,619,136 bytes, SHA-256
`2df0ef0223e22360ba9534d6695ab7413fa4a720f1d2cf4a17ded1f94a28d6e8`. It was

The watcher retrieved `build-ps4-logs/ORBW00009_1792677331.sorbisdmp` (55,064
bytes; SHA-256
`f6c5b3be8fa5efcdc12f109dd735d512aed5607d71a99def54e5a87c8719abaf`). The

Inspection found a likely probe bug: it called `sceKernelDebugOutText` after

that no C/libc/SDK calls happen until host FS is restored. Moved that milestone
log before the FS switch and rebuilt with the ORBW00007 packaging path. The
corrected package is

`c4e951d6b88334a431c95aea4d2f140dd078e7a260a43cbd68425f04bba462a7`); it was

The v2 run also crashed at the same `libkernel.sprx` RIP (`0x800028862`) with
the same `PRX_INVALID_IMAGE` exception. Its dump was captured as
`build-ps4-logs/ORBW00009_1792679737.sorbisdmp` (81,303 bytes; SHA-256
`3a0452e794ab5c691378055c5dd2df769b315c735be271d974bc0bf7d4f17990`). No

The PIE baseline launch succeeded: klog printed `ORBW00009 BASELINE: main

SIGBUS probe itself also contained absolute 32-bit data references that could
not link as PIE. Reworked its compat loop to be inert and let the signal
handler rewrite saved EIP to a local far-return path, avoiding those absolute
references. It now links as `ET_DYN` and is uploaded as
`/data/pkg/orbis-native32-sigbus-probe-pie.pkg` (6,619,136 bytes; SHA-256

That SIGBUS test reached all setup markers, installed the action, created the

`build-ps4-logs/ORBW00009_1792682082.sorbisdmp` (104,291 bytes; SHA-256

ORBW00009 dump watcher is armed again. Install that exact filename and launch
it next; if asynchronous SIGTRAP works, the remaining difference is SIGBUS
delivery, and if it faults at the same libkernel path, async delivery to
compat mode is the broader failure.

The async SIGTRAP control also entered compat mode and faulted at the identical
libkernel RIP `0x80000c776`, read address `0x2c`; no callback ran. Dump

klog confirms the same registers and fault site as the SIGBUS test, so this is

setup, built the same asynchronous SIGTRAP test but kept the receiving thread
in native 64-bit mode. It is uploaded as
`/data/pkg/orbis-native64-async-trap-control.pkg` (6,619,136 bytes; SHA-256

ORBW00009 dump watcher is armed. Install this exact filename and launch it;
success should print `RESULT PASS asynchronous guest-FS signal delivery and

### Follow-up correction: native-64 async signal control (2026-10-22)

The native-64 control received SIGTRAP, executed its handler, and returned
normally. It logged `signal=5 seen=1 unexpected=0 handler_fs=880dac5e0
frame_cs=43 frame_fsbase=880dac5e0 guest_fs=4985e0`. The `RESULT FAIL` line
was only an overly strict assertion expecting compat CS `0x33`; native mode
correctly reports CS `0x43`. Ordinary async signal delivery works in native
mode, but this control did not vary FS and therefore did not isolate compat
CS from guest-FS state.

Built a follow-up that stays in native 64-bit mode but installs the same guest
FS base used in the compat test before receiving asynchronous SIGTRAP. Its
signal context should show CS `0x43` and guest FS. The signal handler rewrites
the saved FS base to host FS before resuming native C, allowing the test to
finish normally. This distinguishes a fault caused by guest FS itself from
one requiring the compat-mode transition.

`10409aa03a5b8064dc87be14c751209958bc1ccd62b97af5ee0899750c319965`.

coredump watcher is running against the persistent klog. Install and launch

The ORBW00010 guest-FS/native-CS control crashed before entering its signal
handler. Its klog shows phase markers through `setting guest FS while remaining
in native 64-bit mode`, followed 0.4 seconds later by SIGSEGV in
`libkernel.sprx` at RIP `0x80000c776`, fault address `0x2c`, with no handler

SHA-256 `10415a36e4fc435e9888b0f2ad392b7e67f6923968725a899f38792671e38301`. Because this run kept CS `0x43` and
only changed FS, the guest FS base alone is sufficient to trigger the same
libkernel failure as the compat tests. This is stronger evidence than the

not compatibility CS itself.

The existing Wine provider patch registers raw kernel sigaction entries
specifically to bypass libkernel's wrapper path. Built the next test to check
whether that path avoids the failure: same guest-FS/native-CS SIGTRAP control,
but installs the action through the validated syscall instruction in the
libkernel `getppid` stub (syscall IDs 20/39 are checked against the imported
`getpid`/`getppid` results before syscall 416 is used for sigaction). Exact
installer filename: `IV0000-ORBW00011_00-ORBW000110000000.pkg`; package
`pkgout/orbis-native64-guestfs-raw-signal-control/`, 6,619,136 bytes, SHA-256
`7236d1967cc1daa0e4258a4e8207263d9912837c21e4a05f3ba38febcc1f9df5`. It is
uploaded to `/data/pkg/orbis-native64-guestfs-raw-signal-control.pkg`, and the
ORBW00011 coredump watcher is armed. Install and launch that exact package
next.

ORBW00011 passed the guest-FS/native-CS SIGTRAP test using raw kernel
sigaction. The klog confirms raw getpid/getppid validation (`155/155`,
`39/39`), then `RESULT signal=5 seen=1 unexpected=0 handler_fs=880dac5e0
frame_cs=43 frame_fsbase=4985e0 frame_rip=4004ce guest_fs=4985e0` and
`RESULT PASS asynchronous guest-FS signal delivery and return`. The normal
libc sigaction control crashed with guest FS, while raw sigaction passed with
the same guest FS. This isolates the failure to the normal libkernel signal
wrapper/entry path; the raw-syscall registration approach in Wine's provider
avoids it for native-CS delivery.

Built the next check with raw sigaction and actual CS `0x33` compatibility
execution, asynchronous SIGTRAP, and the existing far-return recovery. Exact

keeping the capability gate closed until Wine exception/context paths are
validated.

ORBW00012 used raw kernel sigaction in CS `0x33`. Raw syscall validation and
handler installation succeeded, then the async signal returned execution to
the probe's compat recovery stub. The run faulted at EIP `0x4000c2`, the first
`call` in `pw_sigbus_probe_compat_return`, writing to unmapped low address
`0xeeffabcc`. This is a probe-harness stack bug: entering compat code had
truncated the high native stack pointer instead of switching to a mapped low
stack. It did not fault in libkernel and is evidence that raw signal delivery
reached the compat recovery EIP. Dump:
`build-ps4-logs/ORBW00012_1792685139.sorbisdmp` (105,763 bytes, SHA-256
`b0ccfd823bf73e36133bc63dc4f09f60d6cdab2ef5f227bacbf7d22f96ac268a`).

Built ORBW00013 with a dedicated low static compat stack and a saved 64-bit
RSP restored after far-returning to CS `0x43`. Exact installer filename:
`IV0000-ORBW00013_00-ORBW000130000000.pkg`; package
`pkgout/orbis-compat-raw-signal-stack-control/`, 6,619,136 bytes, SHA-256
`573b4630f4a4e87f88b3b4c0d4361b0819ddce889054301879d0db70a263318e`. It is
uploaded to `/data/pkg/orbis-compat-raw-signal-stack-control.pkg`, and its
ORBW00013 coredump watcher is armed. Install and launch this exact package to

ORBW00013 passed the full async SIGTRAP test while executing compat CS `0x33`.
The signal entry restored host FS before C, recorded guest FS `0x4985e0` and CS
`0x33`, rewrote the saved EIP to the low-stack recovery stub, far-returned to
CS `0x43`, and returned to host C. Klog ended with
`RESULT PASS asynchronous guest-FS signal delivery and return`. This validates
the raw syscall registration plus the tested signal frame/return path on this
PS4. The earlier ORBW00012 failure was solely the probe's invalid compat stack.

Porting the proven mechanism into Wine's Orbis provider: its current opt-in
still uses libc `sigaction`, which the ORBW00010/11 A/B shows is unsafe when FS
is the guest TEB. New patch
`wine/orbis/base-patches/0911-ntdll-orbis-native-wow64-raw-sigaction.patch`
validates the `getppid+10` syscall instruction by swapping syscall IDs 20/39
against imported getpid/getppid, then uses syscall 416 with the PS4 kernel's
`handler, flags, 4x32-bit mask` sigaction layout. The capability gate remains
closed unless the build opt-in is enabled and the raw entry validates. The
`ntdll` signal object compiled with the opt-in, and all Wine PRXs linked. The
full host build was skipped after confirming it only rebuilt PE/runtime files
unaffected by this Unix-only patch; cached host artifacts use the same pinned
Wine revision and the same PE-relevant patches.

First ORBW00014 launch reached Wine and verified the native provider at runtime:
the launcher selected `titan-quest-anniversary`, its profile logged `cpu=native`,
Wine logged `PW_NATIVE_INIT accepted version=1 caps=0x00000007 required=0x7`,
and `wow64native initialized: cs32=0033 ss32=003b cs64=0043 avx=1`. The process
remains alive, with no coredump, but has emitted zero frames so far. Startup
also logged `wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create internal thread
context`; investigate this separately as a Titan Quest rendering/startup issue.
This run validates Wine's PS4 signal-provider initialization, not successful
game rendering.

ORBW00014 was then relaunched with the correct `oblivion-gog` profile. The
runtime confirmed `cpu=native`, accepted the native provider capabilities, and
logged `wow64native initialized: cs32=0033 ss32=003b cs64=0043 avx=1`. The user
observed spinning dots for roughly three minutes and closed the run from the
PS4 menu. Throughout the run, `frames_put=0` and sink calls remained at 14;
the process stayed alive and accumulated CPU time. Flexible memory remained
315,392 KiB free, dmem failures were zero, and no coredump was produced. This
is a pre-first-frame native startup stall, not a crash or observed OOM. It is a
regression relative to the fast October 15 behavior, but the current evidence
does not distinguish the raw-sigaction/native WoW64 path from other package
differences. The next useful A/B is the existing
`oblivion-oct15-stick` profile, which explicitly selects the translator on the
same installed package and prefix; compare time to first frame. Keep the
listeners and ORBW00014 dump watcher running.

**ORBW00014 translator A/B (2026-10-22):** On that same installed package and
Oblivion prefix, `oblivion-oct15-stick` logged `cpu=translator prefix_cpu=-2`
and loaded `wowprospero.dll` instead of initializing `wow64native.dll`. It
passed the spinner, submitted its first 326x272 frame at about 11 seconds and
a 1920x1080 frame at about 14 seconds. The preceding native run on the same
package/prefix had remained at `frames_put=0` for nearly three minutes. This
isolates the severe pre-first-frame delay to the native CPU route, rather than
general package startup, game-file access, or shared-prefix state. It does not
yet identify which native transition or guest code path is responsible. Next
step: collect a native guest-PC/thread profile during the early startup window;
do not spend another run waiting for the same uninstrumented stall.

**ORBW00016 first launch (2026-10-22):** Correct guest-PC profile selected;
runtime confirmed `cpu=native`, provider version 3/caps `0x7`, and the profile
environment. The app returned to the launcher after about eight seconds with
Wine's `Unhandled page fault on read access to 00435E70 at address 00435E70
(thread 0024)`. There was no GoldHEN coredump notification. The only native
profile sample was at thread init (`syscall_calls=1`), so the guest-PC/caller
rings were empty and this run did not exercise enough native calls to identify
the earlier spinner. Flexible memory was 360,448 KiB free with no allocation
failures; this is not an OOM signature. Updated the shared profile's Wine debug
channels to include `+seh` (current SHA-256
`02c71c4c115e72755539c7448b2da3f0d0b8d2dad4220d18b4212c33ab064de3`) and
uploaded it to the same profile path. Next: relaunch the same installed
ORBW00016 package with `oblivion-gog-native-guestpc`; capture the `+seh`
exception context and determine whether this fault is caused by the new
sampling pair or is a distinct early native startup fault. Do not build another
package until this context is reviewed.

The `+seh` trace captured the 32-bit exception context: `eip=00435e70`,
`esp=0021fe54`, `ebp=00000025`, `ebx=00000000`, `edi=00000002`, and read fault
address `00435e70`. The console's Wine app exited back to its launcher; no
GoldHEN dump was announced. Retrieved the exact 7,549,440-byte prefix
`Oblivion.exe` by direct RETR from its known path (SHA-256
`035dba38fddd325b3ca18afdaacd086eecf7a8a33fce1abdb057e5cd831e1ee4`). Its PE
image base is `0x00400000`, and `objdump` maps `0x00435e70` to valid `.text`
code (`push -1` at the start of a function). Correction: the captured Windows
exception has `ExceptionInformation[0]=0`, which denotes a **read** access
violation, not an execute violation. Because the fault address also equals the
guest EIP, the available trace is internally ambiguous; do not call this an
instruction-fetch fault or treat the disassembly alone as proof of a relocation
failure. The address is within the image's valid code range, so guest page
mapping/protection and native WoW64 exception context remain open questions. To
isolate the sampler binary,
the already-installed ORBW00014 base now has the same `+seh` channel in
`oblivion-gog-native-profiled.profile` (SHA-256
`ef3c10a4db427db918d4277909c5d955e85d41dd1e367ce0261977e4e8fd6605`, uploaded).
Next run ORBW00014 with that profile and compare: if it returns to the spinner
without the read access violation, the ORBW00016 sampling pair is implicated;
if it faults at the same site, the problem is in the shared native startup path.

**ORBW00018 kernel-RX finding and generic repair (2026-10-22):** The
`00435e70` faulting EIP is inside valid Oblivion `.text` code. Its Windows
`VirtualQuery` record said committed `PAGE_EXECUTE_READ MEM_IMAGE`; that is
Wine's metadata, not proof of the effective PS4 mapping. Earlier October 18
runs at `0x004a8330` showed the kernel mapping could remain R-- after Wine
recorded RX. So the current fault is consistent with the same kernel-RX class,
but ORBW00018 did not capture a live `sceKernelVirtualQuery` result for
`0x00435e70`; retain that distinction until the repaired build logs a verified
mapping.

Replaced the title/address-bounded 0966 experiment with a generic fault-path
repair in `src/wine-patches-ps4/0966-ntdll-repair-native-wow64-image-fetch-page.patch`.
In Wine's `virtual_handle_fault`, it acts only when the fault address equals
the faulting instruction address and Wine's own per-page metadata says that
page is executable. It queries the PS4 kernel, reapplies the existing Wine
protection for that one host page only if the kernel says readable/non-executable,
then retries only after a second query confirms EXEC. Other faults retain the
normal path. It does no startup scan, no per-frame work, and no range-wide
migration; normal execution pays only the existing fault-handler checks. The
first 32 repair outcomes are logged; the recovery is not limited to 32 faults.
This also avoids marking arbitrary data pages executable and removes the
Oblivion-specific address range.

Restored the previously hardware-validated active-stack exclusion as generic
Wine patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0967-ntdll-skip-active-stack-in-force-exec-walk.patch`.
It checks the address of a local on the current Wine stack against each view
in the one-time `virtual_set_force_exec()` startup walk and skips only the
containing view. The guard is `__PS4__`; it adds no per-frame work and does not
change PS5 behavior. Series extension patch 0088 raises the build limit to
0967. Patch checks pass using filtered symlink list
`codex_scratch/wine-patches-0967` (0962–0965 remain excluded because they
conflict with the clean application of the series).
