Wine reports `wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create internal thread
context`. The matching Wine source is `win32u/opengl.c:435`: it fails to find a
pixel format for which the OpenGL driver can create a context, logs the error,
and still returns its allocated context object. This is a real OpenGL context
failure but is not by itself proof of the Titan Quest stall; earlier Pinball
runs logged the same warning without blocking the game. Treat it as a separate
graphics lead until a trace ties it to TQ's startup path.

The same run logs three handled `0xc0000005` faults in
`wineserver.so+0x708f3`, reading `0xffffffffffffffff` during Wine thread
startup. The package's `wineserver.prx` hash matches the local Wine build;
symbolizing the matching `wineserver.shared.elf` maps that offset to
`release_object+0x13` (`server/object.c:640`), an instruction that dereferences
the object's first field. However, the logged register dump shows `RDI` as
`0x5555555555555555`, which does not match the reported fault address. Do not
call this the root cause until a dump or more precise fault-context logging
resolves that discrepancy. The process remained alive well after these faults.

the busy guest thread reported 262,081 syscall transitions, while all sampled
guest-PC and caller entries were zero. This was also seen in the previous
guest-PC experiment, so those arrays are not useful evidence yet. Next
diagnostic should repair/replace that sampling path or capture the relevant

The hot native guest PC resolved against the downloaded console copy of
`thqno_api.dll` (PE base at runtime `0x7a870000`): `0x7a8be6d0` /
`0x7a8be6d5` is RVA `0x4e6d0` / `0x4e6d5`. The stripped DLL disassembly is
conclusive at that site:

```
mov al, byte ptr [ebp-0x15]
test al, al
jne  0x1004e6d0
```

The enclosing function initialized that stack-local byte to 1 at RVA `0x4e605`
and passes its address into a helper before later polling it. The main native
thread repeatedly samples exactly this loop (`CS=0x33`, stable `ESP=0x38fcd4`);
other registered threads remain alive. This is a busy wait for a flag that is
not being cleared, not disk loading, GPU work, or memory pressure. `thqno_api`
is the THQ Nordic Online API DLL, but do not yet conclude which API operation
is waiting or whether the missing signal is caused by Wine thread scheduling.
The DLL was retrieved read-only to `codex_scratch/thqno_api.dll` (6,026,240 B).

Next useful debug step: capture the hot thread's `EBP` and its caller return
address / stack words, and correlate the flag's owner with worker-thread PCs.
Static disassembly or a short instrumentation patch can then identify the
specific THQNO startup operation; only after that should we try a profile or
DLL workaround. Do not spend another run merely confirming the same PC. All
log listeners and the ORBW00019 coredump watcher remain running.

**Titan Quest 10095 stack result (2026-10-22):** The run produced three
matching samples of the spinner's hot loop in `thqno_api.dll` (`RIP`
`0x7a8be6d0`/`0x7a8be6d5`, RVA `0x4e6d0`/`0x4e6d5`) with a complete four-frame
32-bit EBP chain. The first return address is `0x799d0dd1`; subtracting the
runtime `Engine.dll` base `0x79790000` gives RVA `0x240dd1`. The exact console
copy of `Engine.dll` was fetched read-only to `codex_scratch/` and disassembles
that return as the instruction immediately after the call to its `THQNO_Init`
IAT slot. The enclosing exported function is
`?Init@THQNOPlatformInterface@GAME@@UAE_NXZ` (RVA `0x240dc0`). This pins the
stall to Titan Quest's THQ Nordic Online platform initialization, rather than
graphics, file I/O, or general WoW64 startup.

In the stripped console `thqno_api.dll`, the hot code initializes a stack byte
to `1`, passes its address through a worker/task setup, and spins on that byte
at RVA `0x4e6d0`. The associated helper calls MSVC's `_Thrd_start`; the expected
completion signal is not arriving. This is now a concrete async/thread
completion lead. It does **not** yet establish whether the worker fails to
start, blocks in a THQNO operation (possibly networking), or completes without
setting the expected byte. Do not patch out `THQNO_Init` or assume DNS/network
is the cause without one more targeted observation.

The bounded stack sampler worked as intended in 10095; three identical samples
were enough, so the user closed the run. No new package or repeat of the same
launch is needed. Next inspect the THQNO worker callback and its synchronization
path, then design one targeted diagnostic for whether the worker starts and
what it waits on. Console copies of `Engine.dll`, `TQ.exe`, and
`thqno_api.dll` are under the ignored `codex_scratch/` directory; their hashes
and local disassembly correspond to this run.

Follow-up static inspection strengthens the network hypothesis without yet
proving it: the console `libcurl.dll` imports Winsock `getaddrinfo`, and
`thqno_api.dll` contains `nat2.thqonline.net` and `accounts.ea.com`. The Wine
logs repeatedly show `dnsapi:DllMain No libresolv support, expect problems`;
the PS4 Wine configure log confirms the resolver link test failed, so
`dnsapi`'s Unix resolver calls are unavailable. `ws2_32` has a separate
host-`getaddrinfo` route, so the DNSAPI warning alone does not prove the THQNO
request fails. To settle that distinction, the console's
`titan-quest-anniversary.profile` now has
`winedebug = err+all,+loaddll,+process,+dnsapi,+winsock`. This is a profile-only
FTP update (226), with no package rebuild or reinstall. On the next launch of
the existing ORBW00019 package, inspect whether THQNO uses DNSAPI or Winsock
and whether resolver/connect calls return an error before the completion byte
stalls.

**Titan Quest 10095 Winsock trace (2026-10-22):** The follow-up launch supplied
the missing distinction. It did not reach DNS resolution or a remote `connect`:
the trace shows `gethostname()` returning an empty string, followed by
`gethostbyname("")`, then repeated `AF_INET/SOCK_DGRAM` creation attempts.
Each `WSASocketW` path reaches `NtDeviceIoControlFile` with ioctl `0x120320`
(`IOCTL_AFD_WINE_CREATE`); Wine logs a syscall access violation in
`wineserver.so+0x708f3`, the access address is `0xffffffffffffffff`, and
`WSASocketW` returns status `0xc0000005`. A second newly-created Wine thread
logs another syscall fault during this sequence. Meanwhile the original game
thread remains in the verified `thqno_api.dll` `THQNO_Init` completion-byte
spin, with `frames_put=0`. This makes the PS4 Wine AFD socket-creation/syscall
path a concrete failure to inspect before blaming DNS or the remote THQNO
service. It remains unproven that the failed local adapter lookup is the direct
cause of the THQNO wait. No further repeat launch is useful; inspect the
`NtDeviceIoControlFile`/AFD create path and its PS4 syscall boundary first.
The observed server PC offset `0x708f3` resolves against the exact packaged
`wineserver.prx` build's symbol-bearing `wineserver.oelf` to
`pipe_end_destroy` (`server/named_pipe.c:444`); treat this as a lead, not a
root-cause attribution, since this capture has no server-side caller stack.

**10098 result / 10099 fix (2026-10-22):** caller tracing mapped the poison
release to `fd_destroy()` at `server/fd.c:1681`, specifically its final
`fd_owner` release. `fd_destroy()` was reached from the AFD pseudo-fd cleanup;
the pointer was `0x5555555555555555`, Wine's uninitialized-memory poison.
`alloc_fd_object()` initializes `fd_owner` to NULL, but `alloc_pseudo_fd()` did
not. This is a concrete missing-field initialization, not a general PS4
allocator or socket-close problem.

Patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0972-server-initialize-pseudo-fd-owner.patch` sets
`fd->fd_owner = NULL` in `alloc_pseudo_fd()`. The build-series limit is
extended by `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0095-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0972.patch`.
Patch-series validation and the Wine/PRX builds passed. The package-stage
`wineserver.prx` matches the rebuilt PRX byte-for-byte (SHA-256
`c5d05fac82a644b1a07d0d2ca70f364429a5253ae0a25b8c11f54127fe78ae86`), and
the stage contains `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx`.

**10099 native CPU / forced-DX9 run and Engine.dll fault (2026-10-22):** after
the translator DXVK run remained black, changed only
`/data/prospero-win/profiles/titan-quest-anniversary.profile` to `cpu = native`

reported `ready=1`, and the game/engine modules loaded. DXVK 2.6.2 initialized
Vulkan and requested a 1920x1080 windowed D3D9 swapchain. The display path still
reported `frames_put=1`, `shown=0`, `vk_shown=0`; the game never presented a
visible frame. Wine then reported `Unhandled page fault on read access to
41413450 at address 7968CCF0 (thread 0024)` and restarted the Wine launcher.
This is a game-process fault, not a PS4 app/core-dump crash.

Pulled the exact installed `Engine.dll` by direct FTP RETR to
`codex_scratch/ps4-tq-capture/Engine.dll` (3,781,632 bytes). Wine logged its
load base as `0x794C0000`, so the reported PC is RVA `0x1CCCF0`; the file's PE
preferred base is `0x10000000`. Static disassembly at that RVA is
`cmp dword ptr [eax+0x40],0x18`; the reported bad address is exactly
`EAX+0x40`. Added `+seh` to the installed TQ profile's `winedebug` line while
preserving `cpu=native`, `/dx9`, and DXVK. FTP upload and readback succeeded;
remote profile SHA-256 was
`1f55d759a14f4079d48c9fc426581d75f4317bb56dde1f31ad10b11b7ee8fbb1`.

The `+seh` trace adds exception registers: read fault (`info[0]=0`,
`info[1]=0x41413450`), `EIP=0x7968CCF0`, `ESP=0x0038F5D4`,
`EBP=0x0038F670`, `EAX=0x41413410`, `EBX=0x113CDCC0`, `ECX=0x0038F5C0`,
`EDX=0x003B0000`, `ESI=0x7AE15410`, `EDI=0x00D04D30`. The first 64-bit
dispatch context reports `RIP=0x7BEF13A8`, inside packaged `wow64native.dll`
at `+0x13a8`, the `syscall_32to64` entry. That host dispatch frame is where
Wine is handling the exception; the actual faulting instruction is in
`Engine.dll` and directly reads `[EAX+0x40]`. The bad pointer is therefore in
the game-visible call path, not itself evidence of a WOW64 thunk fault. A
DirectSound non-moving-cursor warning appeared about 63 seconds before the AV;
correlation is unproven.

Static analysis of the `Engine.dll` routine beginning at RVA `0x1CCC60` shows
it calling imported `GetAdaptersAddresses`, allocating a buffer through
`MSVCR110!malloc`, calling `GetAdaptersAddresses` again, and then reading the
returned adapter-list object. This makes adapter enumeration the leading
candidate path, but does not yet establish whether the allocator returned an
invalid mapping or the API wrote/returned an invalid pointer. The next run
should query the fault address and `EAX` page state, sample the object's fields,
and capture a bounded EBP chain. Do not change CPU mode, renderer, or memory
policy until that identifies which side produced the bad pointer.

**10101 result: unmapped adapter buffer (2026-10-22):** Installed and ran
10101. Titan Quest again returned to the launcher after the same read fault at
`Engine.dll+0x1CCCF0`; this was not a PS4 app crash. The new `VirtualQuery`
record reports `EAX=0x41413410`, region base `0x41413000`, `AllocationBase=0`,
`state=MEM_FREE`, and `type=0`. Reading the object failed with error 299
(partial copy), as expected for an unmapped region. Flexible memory was still
157,696 KiB, `dmem_failures=0`; this is not an OOM. The single readable frame
has return address `0x7968C464` (`Engine.dll+0x1C464`) and saved EBP
`0x1145F418`, outside the thread stack; the bounded frame walk correctly
stopped there. This proves the game dereferences an unmapped buffer, but not
whether `malloc` returned a stale pointer or `GetAdaptersAddresses`/the native
boundary invalidated it.

Wine's builtin `GetAdaptersAddresses` implementation allocates its own adapter
list, copies into the caller buffer, frees only its internal sorted list and
adapter list, and should not release the caller's buffer. To distinguish a bad
allocation from an API-side unmap, 10102 adds `PW_GAA` traces at API entry and
exit: arguments and input/output size plus `VirtualQuery` state of the caller
buffer before and after the API. This is a diagnostic-only i386 `iphlpapi.dll`
change; it retains the 10101 exception diagnostics, native CPU, `/dx9`, DXVK,
ORBW00019 ID, and relocatable package layout. Source patch:
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0974-iphlpapi-trace-adapter-buffer-state.patch`; patch-series
limit extension:
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0096-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0974.patch`.

staged i386 `iphlpapi.dll` SHA-256 is
`56e35b6eb836cae7edab870372ed20f23c3b8848c1c04b5f65a5d6c3bb141df8`; the

**10102 result and NSI-device trace setup (2026-10-22):** The next Titan Quest
run used package 10102 and reproduced the same launcher exit. Both calls to
Wine's i386 `GetAdaptersAddresses` returned `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` (2),
`count=0`, `size=0`. On the second call, the caller's `malloc` buffer
`0x113CDCC0` was committed before and after the API call with the same
allocation base, so `GetAdaptersAddresses` did not unmap it. The exception is
at the exact `Engine.dll+0x1CCCF0` instruction `cmp dword ptr [eax+0x40],0x18`;
EAX is `0x41413410` while EBX is the live caller buffer `0x113CDCC0`. Thus the
application faults while processing a failed adapter enumeration, and the
register mismatch remains unexplained. The 10102 data is in
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261022-082835.log` (run begins at 16:19:34).

The `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` comes from Wine's NSI path: `nsi.dll` opens
`\\.\Nsi`, and the call fails before NSI enumeration. Direct RETR of the
known prefix registry file confirmed the `nsiproxy` kernel-driver service is
registered as automatic start with image path
`C:\windows\system32\drivers\nsiproxy.sys`. Direct RETR of that exact driver
path succeeded (16,384 bytes); package 10102 also contains the x64 and i386
`nsiproxy.sys` files. This rules out a missing service registry entry or
missing driver file, but does not yet prove whether the service starts or its
device link is created. No FTP listing/probe was used.

Wine already has patch 0740's Vulkan hardware-cursor handoff, and the built
RADV PRX exports `pw_videoout_cursor`. Added
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0975-win32u-trace-vulkan-hardware-cursor.patch`, which
logs only the first cursor state, visibility transitions, and VideoOut result
changes from `ps5drv_vulkan_cursor`. `visible=0` means Wine's driver has no
cursor image to show; `visible=1 result=0` means VideoOut accepted the cursor
image and position; `result=1` means VideoOut is not initialized yet; a
negative result means the hardware-cursor operation failed. Build-series
extension is `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0097-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0975.patch`.

**Titan Quest cursor compositor and package 10108 (2026-10-23):** The user
reached Titan Quest's main menu with package 10107, but no mouse pointer was
visible. Logs established that the Wine Vulkan present hook runs and has a

remain installed and active.

Implemented a small CPU alpha compositor in Mesa's PS4 WSI. Wine's existing
`ps5drv_vulkan_cursor()` passes its transient straight-alpha ARGB cursor image
to the new exported `pw_videoout_cursor`; WSI copies it under a mutex and
blends it into the scan-out buffer after the GPU wait/copy and before
`sceVideoOutSubmitFlip`. Coordinates and cursor size are scaled to the scan-out
size and clipped. The Mesa source lives in the gitignored
`mesa-build/mesa-ps4` tree, so its complete focused change is preserved as
`patches/0012-radv-wsi-cursor-composite.patch`; reverse-apply check passed.
The PS4 PRX build script now includes that export in its descriptor.

`yaml` dependencies. Re-running with
`PYTHONPATH=/usr/lib/python3/dist-packages` completed all 766 Ninja steps and
rebuilt `libvulkan_wsi.a` and `libvulkan_radeon.a`. The standard PRX build
recreated the missing Wine module stubs; then `scripts/radv-prx-ps4.sh`
completed, `create-fself` succeeded, and `llvm-nm -D` confirmed exported
`pw_videoout_cursor`.

Research found the exact Titan Quest AE scaling report: the 2016 Steam thread
says the cursor can disappear/freeze on UHD/4K or Windows scaling at 150% and
above; posters report 100% scaling and restart, or windowed mode, as
workarounds: https://steamcommunity.com/app/475150/discussions/0/343785380901404178/.
This is a Windows-host DPI issue, distinct from our profile's `scaling=fit`
(which is output image scaling). The installed prefix has `LogPixels=0x60`
(96 DPI / 100%) and its TQ.exe AppCompat Layers entry is `HIGHDPIAWARE`, so the
virtual Windows DPI is already at the no-scaling baseline. No registry change
was made. If clicks land offset from the cursor, recheck coordinate scaling;
otherwise continue validating right-stick movement and Cross/R1/Circle clicks.

**Titan Quest network warning (2026-10-23):** After the cursor appeared, the
user reported a “Network problems detected” dialog. This is separate from
cursor presentation. The 10108 run's log shows `GetAdaptersAddresses` returning
the synthetic loopback-only adapter, followed by failed/unsupported name
resolution: `gethostbyname` cannot resolve `gds-tqa-pc.thqonline.net`, and
Wine's `unix_getaddrinfo` says `getaddrinfo() not found during build time` for
`uaa.services.thqonline.net`. The adapter fallback fixed the title's null
adapter crash, but it does not provide external networking or DNS.

A GOG user reports the same dialog in offline single-player, then successfully
switching characters and starting after dismissing it:
https://www.gog.com/forum/titan_quest_anniversary_edition/v29_tq_network_problems_detected.
A Steam thread also documents this warning with offline GOG single-player:
https://steamcommunity.com/app/475150/discussions/0/3759978149434361881/.
Next immediate check: dismiss the warning with Cross and see if offline
single-player proceeds to character selection/game. Do not attribute this to
the cursor or the Mesa compositor. Supporting THQ online services would require
working DNS/network resolution through PS4 Wine's winsock layer, a separate
engineering task.

**PS4 system on-screen keyboard (2026-10-23):** Added a title-side Orbis IME
bridge so games can receive text through the currently focused Wine control.
The player holds Options + touchpad click for one second; the native PS4 IME
opens over the game. Accepting text injects ASCII virtual-key press/release
events into Wine, one character per title tick. Cancel leaves the game field
unchanged. This is available in games launched by the common title, not only
Titan Quest. Non-ASCII IME output is currently skipped because the existing
Wine input bridge represents virtual keys, not Unicode text events. It is
intended for character names and other ordinary Latin text entry.

The bridge loads `ORBIS_SYSMODULE_IME_DIALOG` through `libSceSysmodule`, resolves
`sceImeDialogInit/GetStatus/GetResult/Term`, and uses the active PS4 user ID
from the existing pad service. The system IME interface and struct layout are
in the [OpenOrbis toolchain headers](https://github.com/OpenOrbis/OpenOrbis-PS4-Toolchain/blob/master/include/orbis/ImeDialog.h);
the same two-argument init and result polling flow appears in
[Apollo PS4's IME dialog implementation](https://github.com/bucanero/apollo-ps4/blob/master/source/dialog.c).

(445,513,728 bytes; SHA-256

missing. The new title files are `src/native/pw_osk_ps4.c/.h`; the title build
now links `libSceSysmodule`.

Next hardware check: install the package above, dismiss the unrelated network
warning if it appears, navigate to a text-entry field (for example, the new

PS4 keyboard opens, type a short ASCII name, accept it, and verify the text
appears in the focused Wine field. The app's home-screen identity remains
ORBW00019 / Prospero Wine. Listeners remain running.

**10109 startup stall; isolated OSK build 10110 (2026-10-23):** The first
10109 launch initialized the Orbis IME (`PW_WINE64 osk module=ready`) but stayed
on the spinning dots. The Wine process remained alive, but logged
`frames_put=0`; disk-read counters and fault counts stopped advancing after
startup, so this did not look like the usual slow asset read. Startup also
repeated unsupported `getaddrinfo("127.0.0.1", "9977")` calls. The user closed
that run before reaching a screen where the keyboard could be tested.

Comparison against 10108's known-good `pkg-stage` revealed that the full 10109
rebuild had also replaced i386 and x86_64 `iphlpapi.dll` and i386
`kernelbase.dll`. To remove that confound, built 10110 by repackaging the exact
10108 stage and replacing only `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx` with the
OSK-enabled PRX. The final 10110 stage differs from 10108 only in that PRX and
generated GP4/build metadata. It preserves 10108's eboot, Mesa/Wine modules,
Windows DLLs, and runtime payload. This 10110 package overwrote the remote
`/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00019_00-ORBW000190000000.pkg`; FTP confirmed `226`.

**10110 failed before Wine launch; 10111 fixes the PRX dependency (2026-10-23):**
The first 10110 hardware attempt never reached the launcher. Klog showed the
stub reaching `sceKernelLoadStartModule("/app0/sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx")`
and printing `app PRX load failed`. The OSK PRX's dynamic section adds
`DT_NEEDED libSceSysmodule.so`; the eboot did not depend on that module, so it
was not preloaded when the PRX was started. This was a packaging/loader failure,
not a Titan Quest hang. The stub previously discarded the loader's status, so
it now prints both returned handle and result on failure.

Built 10111 with `-lSceSysmodule` on the eboot link and the status diagnostic,
paired with the OSK PRX from 10109, while preserving the complete 10108 payload.
The verified 10111 stage differs from 10108 only in `eboot.bin` and
`sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx`. It is uploaded to the standard console path
`/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00019_00-ORBW000190000000.pkg` (GoldHEN replied 226).
Local package:
`pkgout/orbis-native-rx-reloc-10111-tq-osk-sysmodule/IV0000-ORBW00019_00-ORBW000190000000.pkg`
(450,625,536 bytes; SHA-256
`9e01622f415fdd53195a25631966749b855f3603b1f7d4dce62899953d7cf9af`).

Next install the package named exactly
`IV0000-ORBW00019_00-ORBW000190000000.pkg`, then launch Prospero Wine and
select Titan Quest. The first check is that the app now gets past the launcher
and reaches the TQ menu. Only after that should the OSK chord be tested: focus a
text field and hold Options + touchpad click for one second. Do not retry 10110;
it is known to fail before Wine starts.

**Physical USB keyboard follow-up, package 10113 (2026-10-23):** The user
confirmed the attached keyboard types in PS4 system UI but not in the game.
That isolates the issue to our title: `pw_hid_ps5_system_ops()` in the PS4
binding returned `NULL`, logging `hid: no PS4 implementation`, despite the
shared Wine HID path already supporting keyboard reports. Replaced the no-op
with PS4 `sceSysmoduleLoadModule` / module-handle lookup for `libSceKeyboard`
(and `libSceMouse`), and resolves device calls with `sceKernelDlsym`. These
modules are preloaded before the title's `/data` credential change. The shared
keyboard state parser's report layout matches the SDK's `OrbisKeyboardData`.

The next launch confirmed `/dx11`, left-stick mouse (`mouse=1`), DXVK 2.6.2,
and the expanded `dxvk.conf` in its effective configuration. The user could

restart, without a package install. The current run began before this profile
change and therefore still had the override; no conclusion about the missing

The current run's runtime heartbeat held `frames_put=267`, `inputs=269`, and
`inblock=4146` from 12:08:54 through 12:12:26, while CPU time and audio kept
increasing. The last memory sample showed 23,552 KiB flexible memory free,
198,656 KiB direct memory (223,168 KiB peak), and zero direct-memory allocation
failures. This rules out continued HDD reads as the immediate stall signal;
memory pressure remains a possible contributor, and shader/pipeline compilation
is now the strongest lead. Do not treat `frames_put` as a DXVK present count.

For the next run, changed the prefix's `dxvk.conf` to retain the expanded HUD
and set `dxvk.enableGraphicsPipelineLibrary = True`; uploaded to

In `build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261023-065929.log`, the active run confirmed the
updated `/dx11` profile, `mouse=left_stick`, and DXVK 2.6.2 reading `dxvk.conf`.
The runtime reached `frames_put=622` / `inputs=629`; those stopped advancing,
and `inblock` leveled at 4120 while CPU time and audio continued. Flexible
memory remained about 27 MiB with zero direct-memory allocation failures.
These are runtime counters, not Vulkan present counters. The last sampled
guest PC in the main TEB was the one-off Engine decoder routine at
`0x794cabd6` (Engine RVA `0x6abd6`); it was not sampled again, so this is not
enough to call it the hang site. Earlier samples confirmed the separate
`THQNO_Init` busy wait at `thqno_api.dll` RVA `0x4e6d0`; that startup loop must
not be conflated with the later black screen.

At 12:29:59 the log records a `0xc0000005` syscall fault for Wine thread
`0000`, at `ntdll.prx+0xc5b2b` / `libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0xbb5f0`, address
`0xfffffff0`; the thread then exited. The runtime heartbeat continued, so
this is a concrete fault to investigate but is not yet proven to cause the
render stall. The klog's later crash report is explicitly for GTA IV
(`LBTY00001`), unrelated to this Titan Quest run; it is not a Titan Quest dump.
Avoid attributing that crash report to the current test. Keep listeners up.

**Post-close mapping of the TQ thread fault:** The packaged `ntdll.prx` has the
same SHA-256 as the linked `ntdll.prx` in `.deps/wine-ps5`, so its ELF symbols
apply. `ntdll.prx+0xc5b2b` is `fstat+0x2b`, the `movaps %xmm0,-0x60(%rbp)`
store while clearing `fstat`'s local `struct stat`. With logged `RBP=0x1000000050`,
that instruction writes to `0xfffffff0`, exactly the reported fault address.
The logged `RSP=0x0fffffff40` is also below the `thread_data` base printed at
thread exit (`0x1000000000`). The same fault address and register pattern recur at
12:00:04, 12:04:56, 12:08:53, and 12:29:59. This is strong evidence that the
the faulting frame is below the logged thread-data base, consistent with stack
underflow or a mismatched stack/data association; it is not evidence that the
boundary mapping itself is wrong. The fstat caller and exact stack identity
are not in the current log, so stack underflow is not yet proven to cause the
TQ render stall. `frames_put` had
already stopped advancing, while the process heartbeat and audio continued.

Next investigation: add a narrowly scoped fault diagnostic recording the
fstat return address (`[RBP+8]`), current thread data/TEB identity, and stack
bounds, then map that caller in the linked server ELF and verify which pthread
supplied the stack. Do not use the stale shader HUD label as a lead.

**Follow-up: TQ syscall stack diagnostic package 10116 (2026-10-23):** Added
Wine patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0980-ntdll-report-server-stack-underflow.patch`.
On an access violation below `get_kernel_stack(data)`, Wine now logs the
thread ID, thread-data and TEB pointers, expected kernel-stack bounds, RSP,
RBP, and a saved return address only when that slot is within the associated
thread-data allocation. It also resolves the return address to a loaded module
when possible. This is diagnostic-only and leaves normal behavior unchanged.

The unfiltered patch directory failed at 0965. After switching to the known-
good filtered set, adding 0973 also failed to apply at `kernelbase/debug.c`;
0973–0978 were therefore left out. The build used
`codex_scratch/wine-patches-0980-final`: the validated 0100–0972 selection
(excluding 0962–0965), followed by 0979 and 0980. Patch-series validation
passed. `build_wine_ps5.sh` reported its documented final nonzero status, but
`dlls/ntdll/ntdll.so: built=True`; the PS4 PRX chain completed. The rebuilt
`ntdll.prx` contains `PW_FAULT_STACK` strings.

Package 10116 was cloned from the exact 10115 package stage. Payload comparison
found exactly two changed files: `sce_module/ntdll.prx` and
`win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/ntdll.prx`. The 1,789-file stage has no additions
or removals, and retains `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx`. Package:
`pkgout/orbis-native-rx-reloc-10116-tq-fstat-stackdiag/IV0000-ORBW00019_00-ORBW000190000000.pkg`
(450,625,536 bytes; SHA-256
`8d4af8b20ec5c90787c731ef993081cd50576133396b83f327e0004392f7d174`). It was
uploaded to `/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00019_00-ORBW000190000000.pkg`; GoldHEN

Install the package with that exact filename and launch Titan Quest. At the
black loading screen, look for `PW_FAULT_STACK tid=... data=... teb=...
stack=... rsp=... rbp=... caller=...` and the following
`PW_FAULT_STACK caller=<module>+<offset>` line in ps5log. If the original

The marker can establish the faulting caller and stack relationship, but it

**10116 test result (2026-10-23):** The user launched Titan Quest, clicked

counters continued rising. Thus this was not a process crash or a no-activity

stall. Keep the listeners running for the next test.

**TQ 10116 syscall-wait diagnosis and 10117 (2026-10-23):** The 10116 log
shows `MSVCR110.dll` and `MSVCP110.dll` loaded from `C:\windows\system32`
as Wine `builtin` modules (for example, at 07:34:46 and 07:34:50). This is
positive evidence that the VC++ 2012 runtime libraries resolved successfully;
the current hang is not explained by missing `msvcr110.dll` / `msvcp110.dll`.
No separate VC redist folder is involved in this package. The log does not
establish that every optional VC++ component is installed, but these two
runtime DLLs are present and loaded.

Offline mapping of the 10116 `PW_NATIVE_PC` samples against
`codex_scratch/libkernel-text-full.bin` identified three hot kernel syscall
return sites: `0x8000013bc` is the post-`kevent` path (syscall 363),
`0x8000027da` is post-`read` (syscall 3), and `0x80000285c` is post-`select`
(syscall 93). In the 13:04–13:05 sample interval, the sites appeared 706,
145, and 107 times respectively. Thus the dominant sampled threads are
waiting/polling in `kevent`, with some `read` and `select`; this is unlike the
earlier THQNO busy-wait at `thqno_api.dll+0x4e6d0`. The syscall entry alone
does not identify which Wine or game caller requested the wait.

post-`read` `RIP=0x8000027da` with `rsp0=0x80000e3d6`, and post-`select`

**Correction to the 10117 caller interpretation (2026-10-23):** The prior
`rsp0` mapping was not a valid caller attribution. With the runtime segment map
from 10117, `0x800c077f1` is offset `0x98ff1` in ntdll, but that offset lands
inside an instruction in `NtShutdownSystem`; it cannot be a return address.
The post-`read` and post-`select` stack values instead point into libkernel
syscall wrappers. Treat the observed syscall paths as real, but discard the
claim that these samples identified their higher-level caller. The sampling
API kept sampled PC/registers in process-global fields, and a signal delivered
to a different thread could overwrite them before the watcher read them. The
sample had no owner identity, so the frame walk could combine registers from
different threads.

Package 10118 adds that missing identity check. The signal handler records the
sampled TEB alongside the PC/register snapshot; `wow64native` logs
`sample_teb=` and `owner_match=` and only walks guest frames when the sampled
TEB matches the watched thread. Patch artifacts are
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0982-ntdll-tag-native-pc-samples-with-teb.patch`,
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0105-wow64native-validate-native-sample-owner.patch`,
and `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0106-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0982.patch`.
Patch 0106 was checked with `git apply --reverse --check` against the current
ignored `prospero-win` build script. The build script's local PE-module list
also includes older `ntdll`/`iphlpapi` entries; those are not part of 0106.

10118's `owner_match=0` records did not prove a cross-thread overwrite. The
signal handler stores `data->teb`, Wine's native TEB, while the first 10118
comparison used `state->guest_fs`, the separate 32-bit guest TEB. In this run
the two were consistently 0x2000 apart. That made the comparison invalid and
the frame walk was correctly suppressed, but it did not answer whether the
sample was overwritten. Patch 0107 fixes the comparison by recording
`NtCurrentTeb()` when each watcher entry registers, comparing that native TEB
to the signal's `sample_teb`, and logging both `native_teb` and guest `teb`:
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0107-wow64native-compare-native-teb-sample-owner.patch`.
The 0107 patch reverse-checks and forward-checks against the current ignored
`prospero-win` source. Rebuilt PS4 PRXs successfully; the new diagnostic string
is present in the staged `wow64native.prx`.

Titan Quest character and pressed Start. The loading screen stopped advancing

dropped at 15:06:43; klog/UDP listeners remain active.

The stack snapshot gives a useful transition clue. Repeated samples show
`RIP=0x800000f7c`, `RDI=0x81`, `RAX=0`, and first stack word `0x7bef1253`.
Disassembly of the captured libkernel text identifies `0x800000f7c` as the
return instruction after syscall `0xa5` (165, `sysarch`); operation `0x81` is
`AMD64_SET_FSBASE`. `0x7bef1253` is the return site immediately after the
`pw_native_sysarch` call in the loaded `wow64native.dll`'s `pw_native_switch_fs`
assembly. This proves the sampled thread was changing FS base through the
native transition path. It does not yet identify which Wine/game caller entered
that path, nor prove this transition caused the graphics hang.

Package 10122 adds four fixed stack words at offsets `0x310`, `0x330`, `0x338`,
and `0x360` from the sampled RSP. These cross the known `pw_native_switch_fs`
assembly frame; the final word should be the return PC of the Wine transition
caller. The read is done in the signal handler only when the full range remains
inside the sampled TEB stack bounds. Patch files are
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0985-ntdll-snapshot-native-transition-caller.patch`,
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0112-wow64native-log-native-transition-caller.patch`,
and `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0113-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0985.patch`.
`build_wine_ps5.sh` applied the full 0985 series; the expected PS5-only PRX
stage skipped because `ps5-native-tool` is absent. All Wine Unix targets and
PE DLLs reported `built=True`; `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` then linked the PS4 PRXs.

**10122 observer-effect confirmation and 10123 profile-off build (2026-10-23):**
The `native_profile=false` launch did suppress `PW_NATIVE_WATCH`/`PW_NATIVE_PC`,
but it still emitted per-thread `PW_NATIVE_PROFILE` reports. Inspection of the
exact `prospero-win/wine/wow64native/unix.c` showed why: the diagnostic patch
hard-coded `profile_enabled = 1`, so each native transition updated profile
counters regardless of the title profile. At 15:30:18–15:37:10 the game
advanced to four submitted frames and then stayed there; by the last heartbeat
it was still alive with `frames_put=4`, CPU time 1,513,130 ms, `inblock=3024`,
and audio continuing. This is a strong observer-effect candidate, not yet
proof that profiling alone caused the graphics stall.

Changed `profile_enabled` to default off and enable only when the profile
explicitly sets `PW_NATIVE_WATCH=1` or `PW_NATIVE_PC=1`. `profile_report()` now
returns immediately when the thread has no profile record. This preserves the
native FS transitions, signal TCB setup, and opt-in diagnostic path while
removing per-transition counter writes from ordinary runs. Patch artifact:
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0114-wow64native-disable-profile-by-default.patch`.

game's Vulkan presents. In `src/wine-patches-ps4/0957-win32u-trace-ps4-vulkan-presents.patch`,
`vk_shown` is documented as counting only the fallback spinner/GDI presenter.
The package already contains bounded WARN logs for successful swapchain
creation and actual `vkQueuePresentKHR` results, but the TQ profile's
`winedebug` filter enabled `err+all` and omitted the `warn` channel. Therefore
the black screen cannot yet be classified as no game present, a failed
present, or a successful present with bad output.

The first profile edit appended `warn+win32u`, but this was the wrong channel:
`dlls/win32u/vulkan.c` declares `WINE_DEFAULT_DEBUG_CHANNEL(vulkan)`. The next
edit used `,+warn+vulkan`; Wine's syntax is `class+channel`, so the leading
`+` made that token a channel name instead of the `warn` class. The resulting
launch did reach `PW_VK_PRESENT cursor hook reached` and `PW_VK_CURSOR
visible=1`, but produced no `PS4 Vulkan` WARN records; do not interpret their
absence as a missing swapchain or present. The run again stalled after Start:
`frames_put=266` at 16:25:53 and still 266 at 16:27:08, with the process alive
(`cpu_ms=517547`, `minflt=111979`, `majflt=2341`, `inblock=4629`,
`audio=21601`, `audible=19558`). The user closed it; runtime disconnected at
16:27:09, with no new crash dump.

Corrected the profile syntax to
`err+all,warn+vulkan,+loaddll,+process,+dnsapi,+winsock` in
`src/profiles/titan-quest-anniversary.profile`; uploaded to
`/data/prospero-win/profiles/titan-quest-anniversary.profile` and read back
byte-for-byte (SHA-256
`39a53bc74bb3c920d36e0e0f183cb2fe0b12ded24cf99a811ebcf47d1cc666c4`). Next
launch the same package/profile (no reinstall), select the existing character,
press Start, then inspect `PS4 Vulkan swapchain` and `PS4 Vulkan present`
records. No swapchain record means creation was not reached; swapchain without
present records means the game stopped before queueing frames; present records
with nonzero results identify the failed WSI operation; repeated successful
present results move investigation to the displayed surface/composition path.
The live listeners stay running. The same log files above contain these runs.

`result=0`, including regular 120-present samples at about 60 Hz. Thus the
prolonged black screen is not a swapchain-create failure or a Vulkan
`vkQueuePresentKHR` error.

The user clicked Start and reported the game hung. Successful presents
continued through #1560 at 16:30:29.574, then no further `PS4 Vulkan present`
calls appeared before close at 16:31:47.680. No swapchain recreation occurred.
At the final heartbeat Wine was still alive with `frames_put=229`,
`inputs=237`, `cpu_ms=552391`, `minflt=113640`, `majflt=2341`, `inblock=4599`,
and `audio=23388`; this app-level `frames_put` counter is not the Vulkan
present count. The useful boundary is that TQ stops calling QueuePresent after
the Start transition while Wine/audio remain active. This localizes the stall
upstream of `win32u_vkQueuePresentKHR` in the game's render/transition path;
it does not yet identify whether the game thread is blocked on resource,
shader, or CPU work. Runtime disconnected at 16:31:47.680; no crash dump.

The launch-to-first-swapchain delay was about 94 seconds, mostly before DXVK
initialized. This is a real testing cost and should be tracked separately
from the post-Start render stall. The 1-thread DXVK setting remains in the TQ
prefix and no reinstall was needed. The corrected profile remains on the
console. The live listeners remain running with the same runtime/klog/UDP log
files listed above.

**10124 closed-run inference and 10125 Vulkan boundary trace (2026-10-23):**
The user closed 10124 after more than a minute with no visible progress. The

swapchain image acquisition, continuing command submission without presenting,
or looping in its own transition/render code.

against a later, fully applied file and did not apply at their own sequence
positions. Both were re-anchored at the correct incremental locations. The
full series then replayed through 0986. `build_wine_ps5.sh` completed all
required targets with `built=True` but returned its known nonzero status due
duplicate PS4 heap/dmem glue symbols in the exploratory ntdll link; the PS4
linker script `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` then successfully rebuilt all PRXs,
including `win32u.prx`.

RADV's final budget window before the freeze had 188 submits / 94 presents over
7.847 seconds. The following 5.563-second window had four submits, zero
presents, zero render passes/draws, and zero GPU-wait time. CPU use was still
about 3.9 cores average. The visible frozen picture plus continuing audio is
therefore a graphics-progress stall, but these counters do not establish a GPU
hang: at that point Mesa recorded no present calls or rendered work. The
`hung-ring slot` warnings for submits #3990 (32,344 dwords) and #3995 (16,576
dwords) only say those command streams exceeded the diagnostic capture buffer;
they are not submission failures or proof that either submit caused the stall.

Correction to the initial sampler read: the run did capture guest 32-bit
execution. Of 4,645 `PW_NATIVE_PC` samples during the 12:27–12:30 window,
4,511 carried `CS=0x43` and 42 carried `CS=0x33` (the remaining records had no
CS field). The startup `TQ.exe` thread `teb=7ffda000` repeatedly hit the already
known `thqno_api.dll+0x4e6d0` wait before the menu; do not treat that as the
post-Start fault. During the frozen interval, sparse guest samples on
`teb=7ffce000` instead landed at several different PCs with stack frames inside
GOG's `Galaxy.dll`; two other guest samples landed in `Engine.dll` at `+0x172117`
and `+0x6abd6`. Disassembly maps the first Engine site to scalar-float math and
the latter to a table-driven bitstream decoder. These isolated samples do not
prove either routine is stuck. The 10129 sampler did not give enough per-thread
CPU accounting to say which guest thread consumed the roughly four active
cores.

**10130 result and 10131 follow-up (2026-10-24):** After selecting Titan Quest
with 10130, the profile loaded and the native CPU profile reached version 3.
About 85 seconds later the game returned to the launcher. Wine logged an
unhandled read fault in thread `0024`: attempted address `DB23DB62`, exception
address `7995CCF0`; Wine then tried to start `winedbg`, which cannot create a
process on this PS4 route. The app did not produce a GoldHEN coredump or
`coredump.path` notification; the klog shows the Wine process exit and launcher
restart. The captured log is `build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261023-065929.log`.

The exact console `Engine.dll` maps `7995CCF0` to `Engine.dll+0x1CCF0`. Its
captured bytes disassemble as `mov ecx, esi`, which cannot itself read
`DB23DB62`. Thus the current exception line alone is internally inconsistent;
we need the fault-time 32-bit register context and stack before attributing this
to the game or Wine. Source already had `PW_EXCEPTION32` logging, but the
installed 32-bit `kernelbase.dll` did not contain it. Built that PE DLL from
the patched Wine source, verified the markers, and made package 10131 include it
through `PS4_KERNELBASE32_DLL`.

stack. Keep ps5logd 9300, UDP 18194, and klog 3232 running.

**10131 result (2026-10-24):** The user installed 10131, launched Titan Quest,
and it returned to the launcher. The exception-context build worked. The fault
was a read of `DB23DB62` with `EAX=DB23DB22` at `EIP=7994CCF0`; the module map
places that instruction at `Engine.dll+0x1CCF0` (`Engine.dll` loaded at
`79780000`). I downloaded the exact console `Engine.dll` after the run; its
SHA-256 is `0aedbb1805b4a5616f74e34d4f609f392e2c2dd4561c64c118f4772ab4f694f6`.
It matches the earlier console copy and its bytes at that RVA match the runtime
instruction `cmp dword ptr [eax+0x40], 0x18`.

The containing exported function is
`GAME::ConnectionManager::GetLocalIpAddresses`. It dynamically resolves and
calls `GetAdaptersAddresses`, then reads the adapter structure's `Length` field
at offset `0x40`. This ties the crash to local network adapter enumeration.
Immediately before the fault, Wine logged Winsock startup, UDP socket setup,
and `gethostname` returning an empty name. The syscall-fault logger reports a
fault in `libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0x4f069` while syscall `0x3f` was active.

Do not yet call this an IPHLPAPI buffer bug: at the fault `EBX=1148EE40` while
`EAX=DB23DB22`, and the latest run has no `PW_GAA` trace adjacent to the fault.
The exception PC and bad pointer establish where the game dereferenced invalid
state, but not whether the cause is its dynamically resolved adapter API,
Wine's adapter enumeration, or native syscall context preservation. Preserve
the log at `build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261023-065929.log`.

The missing trace is explained by the package source split: packages stage
their PE modules from `.deps/wine-host/install`, while the PS4-specific
`iphlpapi` changes are built under `.deps/wine`. Package 10131 therefore
contained the generic host `iphlpapi.dll`; its SHA-256 was
`8e8805dffeaf987d3dc0067b565301a1dd56b03bac8ad5b389a51239ebe27631` and it
lacked both `PW_GAA` and the PS4 NSI fallback. The PS4 build contains those
changes. Added an opt-in `PS4_IPHLPAPI32_DLL` packaging override, rebuilt the
i386 module, stripped it, and verified the staged DLL matches the intended
file and contains both markers.

**10132 Titan Quest run and Winsock resolver gap (2026-10-24):** The user
installed 10132 and launched the same Titan Quest profile. It stayed at
spinning dots, with `frames_put=0` throughout; the process remained alive and
disk-read counters stopped changing. The 10132 log showed the game entered
`GAME::ConnectionManager::GetLocalIpAddresses` (so retain the 10131/10132
adapter-fault investigation), but no `PW_GAA` line was emitted before this
spinner run ended.

The same run produced repeated definitive Winsock diagnostics:
`unix_getaddrinfo getaddrinfo() not found during build time` for both
`uaa.services.thqonline.net` and `127.0.0.1:9977`. The PS4 PRX build's generated
`.deps/wine-ps5/build/include/config.h` has `HAVE_GETADDRINFO` undefined, so
Wine's `ws2_32/unixlib.c` compiles its `#else` path, which returns
`WS_EAI_FAIL`. This is a real resolver implementation gap, not evidence that
the console's internet is down. Package 10132 only replaced the i386
`iphlpapi.dll` and retained the same `ws2_32.prx`; it could not fix this path.

The upstream PS5 helper `prospero-win/wine/ps5/pw_ws2_32_libc.c` was purposely
limited to numeric addresses, wildcard/loopback, and `localhost`/the console
hostname. That policy is unsuitable as the final PS4 behavior: Titan Quest is
calling DNS for THQNO endpoints, and its local `127.0.0.1:9977` lookup also
currently fails because the PS4 PRX doesn't have the helper. Do not treat this
as a DNS-only symptom or claim it fully explains the no-frame hang: the
already-measured THQNO worker still spins in its init path, and we have not yet
proved the causal link.

The OpenOrbis PS4 SDK provides `libSceNet.so` and exports the resolver API;
its `Net.h` declares `sceNetResolverCreate`, `sceNetResolverStartNtoa`, and
`sceNetResolverDestroy` (the SDK's older Aton declarations are unprototyped).
The PRX linker already links against `-lSceNet`. Next implementation task:
add a PS4 Winsock resolver backed by `sceNetResolver` for IPv4 name lookups,
retaining fast numeric/loopback answers, with bounded resolver timeouts and
correct Winsock `addrinfo`/error mapping. Then package and verify a fresh
`unix_getaddrinfo` trace and whether the THQNO worker advances beyond the
spinner. Do not rely on FTP probing; logs come from the existing listeners.

**10133 PS4 Winsock DNS resolver (2026-10-24):** Added an opt-in
`PW_PS4_DNS` path to `prospero-win/wine/ps5/pw_ws2_32_libc.c`. Numeric,
wildcard, and loopback answers stay local and immediate. Other IPv4 names now
use `sceNetPoolCreate` + `sceNetResolverCreate/StartNtoa/Destroy`, with a
one-second resolver timeout, one retry, a process-local cache (60 seconds for
success, three seconds for failure), and one diagnostic line per cache fill:
`PW_SCE_NET_DNS host=... result=... sce=... ip=...`. The 32-bit/title APIs
continue to use Wine's existing Winsock conversion and error mapping.

The PS4 PRX build now compiles `ws2_32/unixlib.c` with `HAVE_GETADDRINFO`
enabled, since the generated PS4 `config.h` cannot discover the console-only
resolver, and links in the resolver shim. An initial link omitted that object
because the script replaced `shim_objs`; corrected the script and verified the
final `ws2_32.prx` has `getaddrinfo`, `freeaddrinfo`, and `getnameinfo`, imports
the four expected `sceNet*Resolver/Pool` functions from `libSceNet`, includes
the `PW_SCE_NET_DNS` diagnostic, and no longer contains the
`getaddrinfo() not found during build time` fallback.

Added `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0118-wow64native-report-active-syscall-samples.patch`.
Every two seconds, for each native thread that used over 100 ms of CPU, the
watcher now prints the rolling 16-entry guest syscall PC, syscall ID, and first
syscall argument samples as `PW_NATIVE_ACTIVE_SYSCALLS`. The field is named
`arg1` deliberately: the inherited `syscall_caller_samples` ring reads
`[guest ESP+4]`, which is the first argument, not a return address. This is
bounded diagnostic logging; it does not change the native-backend gate or
runtime selection. The PE CPU DLL and PS4 Unix PRX were rebuilt as a matched
ABI-v3 pair: DLL SHA-256 `5fa591df529a73089023751e8a35b14356d54ffd5938f03d351f0e4fff13d53f`; PRX SHA-256 `022db12042ccfb1175e00a4bff920efea2f9cebc8480f78657c8fcd06665ed61`.

**10136 live result and caller-sample correction (2026-10-24):** The user
launched 10136 and reported a black screen. Its heartbeat stayed alive with
`frames_put=1`, `shown=0`, and no Vulkan presents. Native TEB `7ffce000`
continued making `NtQueryPerformanceCounter` calls (`id=0x31`), reaching
432,000 dispatched syscalls during the observed interval. The guest PC
`0x7bf2d580` maps to the i386 `ntdll` syscall thunk for that service. The
sample labelled `arg1=0x7bf5013b` was misidentified: with the exact packaged
i386 `ntdll.dll` loaded at `0x7bf20000`, that address maps to the instruction
after the syscall call inside the exported `RtlQueryPerformanceCounter`
wrapper. The 10136 `syscall_caller_samples` ring therefore held a wrapper
return PC, not the syscall's first argument. This identifies a hot QPC path,
but not yet the higher-level game caller or the root cause of the black frame.

**10137 first-frame observation and Galaxy caller mapping (2026-10-25):** The
user launched 10137 and reported a black screen while waiting for the startup
animations. At 09:29:59 the Wine heartbeat still showed `frames_put=1`,
`shown=0`, `vk_shown=0`; the process remained alive. The native thread
`teb=7ffce000` had reached 131,079 syscalls, with repeated `NtQueryPerformanceCounter`
(`id=0x31`) samples and `caller=0x7874402e`.

That caller maps to the loaded `Galaxy.dll` base `0x78230000`, RVA `0x51402e`.
The exact console DLL already retrieved as
`codex_scratch/tq-galaxy-console.dll` disassembles at that RVA to the
instruction immediately after an indirect call through IAT slot RVA
`0xa5a178`; `objdump -p` identifies that import as `QueryPerformanceCounter`.
This establishes that a Galaxy code path is polling QPC during startup, but it
does not establish that Galaxy is blocking rendering or causing the missing
animation. The user is preparing a no-Galaxy copy for an A/B run; compare its
startup frames and caller/syscall samples against this baseline before
attributing the delay.

**Startup timing caveat:** The 10137 profile has `native_profile=true` and a
verbose `WINEDEBUG` value (`err+all,warn+vulkan,+loaddll,+process,+dnsapi,+winsock`).
The native profile enables watch, PC sampling and per-thread syscall logging.
The `-g` debug metadata in the instrumented DLL is not evidence of symbol
loading overhead by itself; the active sampling and Wine logging can add
measurable overhead. Do not use 10137 as the startup-performance baseline.
After the no-Galaxy comparison, use the same runtime with native profiling
disabled and minimal Wine logging to isolate diagnostic overhead while leaving
the compiled code and debug sections unchanged.

navigation. Such a build also enables automatic launcher/game cycling, so

**Titan Quest NetCtl adapter package 10138 (2026-10-25):** Replaced the
loopback-only `GetAdaptersAddresses` fallback with a PS4 NetCtl snapshot. The
native title initializes/queries `sceNetCtlGetInfo` for IPv4 address, netmask,
default route, primary/secondary DNS and MTU, then publishes one
`WINE_PS4_NETCFG` variable into the Wine PRX environment before `__wine_main`.
When NSI remains unavailable, Wine now reports an `orbis-ethernet` Windows
adapter with those actual fields and linked unicast/gateway/DNS records; the
loopback fallback remains only when NetCtl has no usable IPv4 address. DNS
resolution already worked through `sceNetResolver`; this targets applications
that gate initialization on adapter enumeration. It does not fake a service on
localhost: Titan Quest's repeated 127.0.0.1:9977 connection refusals remain a
separate condition (there is no listener on that port).

Source changes: `src/native/pw_netctl_ps4.[ch]`, the app link includes
`-lSceNetCtl`, `prospero-win/native/wine64_main.c` publishes the snapshot, and
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0989-iphlpapi-use-ps4-netctl-adapter.patch` builds both
Wine PE architectures. Patch-series limit extended in
`prospero-win/tools/build_wine_ps5.sh` and captured by
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0120-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0989.patch`.
The NetCtl helper compiled with the PS4 compiler; the series check passed; Wine
reported both `iphlpapi.dll` architectures built, and all PS4 PRXs plus the
relocatable base package built. `build_wine_ps5.sh` still exits 1 after
reporting `built=True` for the expected modules, per the existing build-script
behavior.

**10140 resolver fault root cause and 10141 (2026-10-25):** The 10140
launch still produced no frame and faulted in `ws2_32.prx` during the empty
`gethostbyname` path. Mapping the faulting instruction against the exact
`ws2_32.shared.elf` showed `__tls_get_addr` returned NULL; `pw_hostent()` then
wrote a field at TLS offset `0x130`. This is why the `PW_PS4_LOCAL_HOST`
marker never appeared: the fault happened while constructing the `hostent`,
after entering the new resolver helper but before its return log. The code
path is being used; the previous interpretation that it was bypassed was wrong.

Changed `pw_ws2_32_libc.c` so the PS4 PRX build uses static resolver/hostent
storage rather than unsupported C TLS; non-PS4 builds retain thread-local
storage. Rebuilt `ws2_32.prx`, confirmed the shared ELF has no TLS relocations,
and made 10141 from the 10140 stage, replacing only the two Winsock PRX copies.
Thus the display payload remains byte-identical to the known-good 10124 build,
and the NetCtl app, adapter DLLs, and all other 10140 payloads remain unchanged.

After the user pressed Start, the same stall remained and the app crashed at
11:24:41. The klog shows Vulkan `vkQueuePresentKHR` returning `VK_SUCCESS`
through present #3360, so this was not the earlier no-present symptom. Wine
was still alive with audio advancing; the app-level frame/input counters rose
to 307 and then stopped. At 11:24:40 the PS4 captured SIGSEGV in
`ntdll.prx`; the retained dump is
`codex_scratch/orbw00019-coredumps/ORBW00019_1792952680.sorbisdmp` (382,490
bytes, SHA-256
`4b3f5da8e38b438dd2822c570015ca92f390266c1be50d451fa30a364959a910`). The
FACECS dump container remains opaque, but the klog's module map and the exact
10142 build's `ntdll.so` symbols decode the signal path: `ntdll+0xc613b` is
`mprotect_range()` / inlined `get_host_page_vprot()`, and repeated
`ntdll+0xc7782` frames are `virtual_setup_exception()`. A frame at
`ntdll+0xba2af` maps to `NtCreateThreadEx()`. The backtrace points to a
recursive Wine exception/stack-protection failure; it does not yet identify
the first exception that started it. Immediately before the signal, klog was
reporting executable-range flex migrations. The last memory sample showed
`flexible_free=31,744K`, `dmem=171,200K`, and `dmem_failures=0`, so exhaustion
is not established.

Conclusion: 10142 confirms that the network dialog was one real issue, but
fixing it did not fix the post-Start stall. Keep the crash dump and both logs.
The next diagnostic should capture the first exception context and stack bounds
before `virtual_setup_exception()` recurses, and correlate it with executable
range migration / thread creation. Do not repeat the same 10142 run as if it
were a new test. Keep the runtime, UDP, klog, and coredump listeners running.

**10143 first-exception trace built and uploaded (2026-10-25):** The 10142
crash is captured and mapped to Wine's recursive `virtual_setup_exception()` /
`mprotect_range()` path, but its dump does not expose the first exception. Added
`PW_EXCEPTION_SETUP`, a PS4-only `ERR` record at the start of
`setup_raise_exception()`. It logs the exception record and parameters, guest
and signal RIP/RSP, and the TEB stack base/limit/deallocation stack. The atomic
counter caps this at the first 24 events so a recursive fault cannot flood the
log. This is diagnostic only; it does not change exception handling.

Review found a safety problem in the existing raw signal diagnostic: after
printing RSP, it unconditionally reads five words from that interrupted stack.
The recorded RSP is `0x0000000fffffff40`, so it is not safe to dereference. The
log contains the raw register line but no stack-word records, and the page-fault
boundary trace is absent. This does not prove that the diagnostic caused the
fatal fault, but it could fault in the handler and prevent later trace code from
running. Patch
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0993-ntdll-stop-reading-faulting-signal-stack.patch`
removes those untrusted stack reads while preserving the raw register record;
the active patch copy is `prospero-win/wine/patches/0993-ntdll-stop-reading-faulting-signal-stack.patch`.
Patch `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0124-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0993.patch`

Re-reading the 10146 Titan Quest trace corrected an earlier interpretation:
`virtual_handle_fault()` returns an `NTSTATUS`, not a boolean. Its logged value
`-1073741819` is `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` (`0xc0000005`), so the two recorded
faults were not handled. The first fault was a read at `0xffffffff0` with
`RSP=0xfffffff40`, `thread_data=0x1000000000`, and `data->teb == NULL`; the
second fault read at `0xfffffff38`. Afterward, the app crashed at `0x48`,
`R14=0x1000000000`. With ntdll mapped at `0x800b0c000`, that RIP resolves to
`setup_raise_exception+0x57`. The 0991 `PW_EXCEPTION_SETUP` logger dereferenced
`data->teb` unconditionally, so that diagnostic likely caused the secondary
0x48 crash and masked what `setup_raise_exception()` would have done for the
original access violation. This is a logger bug; the original invalid-stack
fault remains unresolved.

**10147 Titan Quest fault mapping and stack trace package 10148 (2026-10-25):**
The user pressed Start in Titan Quest. Runtime log
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261023-065929.log` recorded a first access violation at
15:20:00: `ntdll.prx+0xc642b`, write to `0xffffffff0`, with `RSP=0x0fffffff40`,
`RBP=0x1000000050`, `thread_data=0x1000000000`, and no TEB. The reported
kernel stack range was `0x1000010000-0x1000110000`, so the interrupted stack was
below its assigned range. The previous source lookup against `ntdll.so` used
the wrong address layout; symbolizing against the packaged PRX's linked ELF
`prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/prx/ntdll.shared.elf` confirms the instruction is
`fstat+0x2b`, a local stack write. Its return address `ntdll.prx+0xba59f` is
`pw_compat_futimens+0x4f`, immediately after the shim's `fstat()` call. This
places the failure in file timestamp handling with an already-invalid host
stack; it does not establish that the shim caused the stack error.

38 ms later the signal path faulted again in `libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0xbb5f0`
at `0xffffffff38`. The null-safe `PW_EXCEPTION_SETUP n=1` now logged this
second fault without itself crashing. The process continued reporting audio,
but its frame count remained at 281. Seven seconds later another access
violation occurred in `wow64native.so+0x1b058`, symbolized in the linked ELF as
`profile_watch_register+0x168`, with fault address `0x53ac`. Treat this as a
separate profiler-path fault pending a cleaner capture. The ps5log stream
disconnected at 15:20:13; the user switched to Oblivion and confirmed it was
loading, so this was a cross-title check rather than a new Titan Quest result.

At 15:38:18 the recurring fault still occurred on a different thread-data

`param=0x800bc673f`. The bounded frame chain stayed in the low thread-data

kernel stack. Do not interpret these `start`/`param` values as a proven
callback: unlike the logged `tid=007c` thread, the faulting data block has no
saved pthread id or TEB, and these fields alone do not establish which thread

`param` as `create_server_thread()` does, so the values may be stale or
otherwise not meaningful for callback attribution. The fault maps again to

stack surfaced, not proof that file timestamp handling caused it.

**10149 initial-thread stack startup trace (2026-10-25):** The 10148 linked
ELF disassembly confirms the first fault instruction is `fstat+0x2b`,
`movaps %xmm0,-0x60(%rbp)`. Its effective write address is
`RBP(0x1000000050)-0x60 = 0xffffffff0`, exactly the recorded access-violation
address. The stack pointer is `0xfffffff40`, 0xc0 bytes below the recurring
thread-data allocation at `0x1000000000`, while the reported 1 MiB pthread
stack starts at `0x1000010000`. That pins the immediate failure on a frame
straddling the low edge of the 64 GiB thread-data mapping; it is not evidence
of a bad file descriptor or an `fstat()` filesystem error. The shim is still
the point where the invalid frame faults, not yet the origin of the stack
switch/corruption.

**10154 UMTX fields (2026-10-25):** Added `src/wine-patches-ps4/0998-ntdll-snapshot-waiting-umutex-fields.patch`
and patch-series extension `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0129-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0998.patch`.
The native signal sampler now snapshots four aligned 32-bit words at the
sampled syscall's `RDI` only for syscall `0x1c6`, operation `0x11`, after
checking the pointer is aligned and in the lower canonical user address range.
For the FreeBSD `struct umutex` layout these are `m_owner`, `m_flags`, and the
two priority ceilings ([FreeBSD `_umtx.h`](https://cgit.freebsd.org/src/tree/sys/sys/_umtx.h?id=8f1a2d507e25e77d20a5d7675dc8eee9b83d3570)).
The opt-in `PW_NATIVE_PC` log emits them as `umtx_words=[...]`. Patch
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0130-wow64native-log-sampled-umutex-fields.patch`
passes those fields to the logger and preserves the earlier `rsp_words`
capture. The ordered Wine patch series checks through 0998; the full builder
reported `ntdll.so: built=True` and ended at the expected PS5-only PRX stage.
PS4 PRXs were then rebuilt through `scripts/prx-ps4.sh`, RADV, FreeType, and
WinePS5; the `wow64native.prx` contains the new formatter and `ntdll.prx`
exports `__wine_prospero_native_profile_sample`.

the Wine host had fullscreen while the earlier PS4 file used borderless. A
DXVK issue documents a D3D11 fullscreen-mode failure when DXGI cannot match the
requested display mode, with a later fix, so this remains a relevant A/B
variable but is not proven causal here. Current options still differ in
`resolution` (PS4 1920x1080 vs Wine 1024x768) and `showIntro` (PS4 false vs
Wine true). The current PS4 run is reported to fail while loading the character
model. Runtime remains alive with `frames_put=71` and `shown=0` unchanged for
over a minute while `audio` continues past 37,000; no new core dump has
appeared. An earlier `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` call entered at #331. Treat this
as a render or presentation stall unless the Wine process exits or a dump
arrives. Do not change more settings during this run.

**10160 sampled request identified; 10161 server-side mapping trace (2026-10-26):**
A closer decode of the 10160 native sample resolves the blocked read: the sampled
registers are hexadecimal, so `rdi=0x22` is file descriptor 34, and the request
sequence field identifies Wine server request 63, `create_mapping` (not a
filesystem read). The thread was waiting for its 64-byte server reply. The
previous entry above that called this an unidentified fd 22 should be read as
fd 34 / `create_mapping`; the Wine server source confirms request ID 63.
